(ii) homogeneity, $\max(|t|N(v_1), |t|N'(v_2)) = |t|\max(N(v_1), N'(v_2))$; (iii) the triangle inequality, each component's inequality bounded by the sum of the maxima; (iv) completeness: a sequence Cauchy for the maximum is Cauchy for each component, the components converge to $a$ and $b$ by completeness of $N$ and $N'$, and the sequence converges to the pair $(a, b)$ for the maximum. *Condition (2) of Definition 5.1.1*: if the base points $P(w_k)$ converge to $x$ and $(N \times N')(w_k) \to 0$, then both $N(w_{k,1}) \to 0$ and $N'(w_{k,2}) \to 0$ by squeezing between $0$ and the maximum, condition (2) for $N$ and $N'$ gives $w_{k,1} \to 0_{x_1}$ in $W[1]$ and $w_{k,2} \to 0_{x_2}$ in $W'[1]$, and the inverse of the homeomorphism of the $[1]$-levels carries the pair to $w_k \to 0_x$ in $(W \times W')[1]$. *Over the ep-groupoid* ⟦Chapter15/ProductAuxNorm · EpStrongBundle.isAuxNorm_prodAuxNorm⟧ (Definition 8.3.5): in addition $N \times N'$ is invariant under $\mu \times \mu'$, because each component is invariant under its own structure map.

**The product problem and Remark 15.3.4** (same) ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.IsAdmissible.prodMult⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_4⟧. *Statement.* Let $f$, $f'$ be sc-Fredholm section functors with tame germ data and $\Lambda$, $\Lambda'$ sc⁺-multisections, $(N, U)$- and $(N', U')$-admissible. Then $f \times f'$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor with tame germ data, $\Lambda \times \Lambda'$ is an sc⁺-multisection, $(N \times N', U \times U')$-admissible for $f \times f'$, and its domain support lies in $(U \times X') \cup (X \times U')$. *The multisection.* The strong bundle is the product over the product ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SEP.prod⟧ and $(\Lambda \times \Lambda')(h, h') = \Lambda(h)\Lambda'(h')$ ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.prodMult⟧. Local section structures multiply: if $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ represents $\Lambda$ on $O$ and $(s'_j, \sigma'_j)$ represents $\Lambda'$ on $O'$, the $kk'$ sections $s_i \times s'_j$ with weights $\sigma_i\sigma'_j$ represent $\Lambda \times \Lambda'$ on $O \times O'$, since the number of $(i, j)$ with $s_i \times s'_j$ through $(h, h')$, weighted, is the product of the two weighted counts ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.IsLocalRepOn.prodMult⟧; the sections are sc⁺ by `IsScPlusSection.prod` above, so $\Lambda \times \Lambda'$ is an sc⁺-multisection ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.IsScPlusMultisection.prodMult⟧. *Norm, scaling, admissibility.* The pointwise norm of $\Lambda \times \Lambda'$ at $(x, x')$ is a maximum over pairs of branches, hence $\max(N(\Lambda)(x), N'(\Lambda')(x'))$ ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_prodMult⟧, and $t(\Lambda \times \Lambda') = (t\Lambda) \times (t\Lambda')$ for every real $t$ ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.multisectionSmul_prodMult⟧. So the norm is $\le 1$, and with the domain support below the product is admissible; $(N \times N', U \times U')$ controls compactness when $(N, U)$ and $(N', U')$ do, since $\{(N \times N')(f \times f') \le 1\} \cap (U \times U')$ is the product of the two sets, whose images in the orbit spaces have compact closures ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.ControlsCompactness.prod⟧. *Remark 15.3.4.* $(\Lambda \times \Lambda')(x, x')$ is the trivial multisection exactly when both factors are, so the domain support is $(\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}\,\Lambda \times X') \cup (X \times \mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}\,\Lambda')$ ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.domSupp_prodMult⟧, which lies in $(U \times X') \cup (X \times U')$. The inclusion is sharp: if the domain support lies in $U \times U'$ and $\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}\,\Lambda \ne \emptyset$, then $U' = X'$ ⟦Chapter15/ProductMultisection · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_4_eq_univ⟧. *Sc-Fredholm.* The germ notions of Part I multiply: neighbourhood germs ⟦Chapter15/ProductGerms · ScNbhdGerm.prod⟧, filling germs, with the kernel of the product filler the product of the kernels ⟦Chapter15/ProductGerms · IsFillingGerm.prod⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ProductGerms · kerProdCLE⟧, basic germs after reordering the coordinates to $(P \times P') \oplus (W \oplus W')$ ⟦Chapter15/ProductBasicGerm · IsBasicGerm.prod⟧, and conjugacy to basic germs through the product of the strong bundle isomorphisms ⟦Chapter15/ProductBasicGerm · IsStrongBundleIso.prod⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ProductBasicGerm · IsConjugateToBasicGerm.prod⟧. Hence $f \times f'$ is sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter15/ProductFredholm · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSection.prod⟧ with tame germ data ⟦Chapter15/ProductFredholm · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionTame.prod⟧, an sc-Fredholm section functor ⟦Chapter15/ProductFredholm · EpStrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionFunctor.prod⟧, and $|S_{f \times f'}|$ is compact when $|S_f|$ and $|S_{f'}|$ are ⟦Chapter15/ProductFredholm · SBLocalization.hasCompactSolutionSetEp_prodMap⟧. No hypothesis beyond the factors' properties enters; every pair of the multisection examples of the library is an instance.

### XV.20 Definition 15.3.5 and Remark 15.3.6 (2017: Definition 15.6, Remark 15.6): general position

**Definition 15.3.5** (restated) ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPosition⟧. *Statement.* $(f, \Lambda)$ is *in general position* if for every $x \in X_1$ with $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$, every operator $L$ in the support of the formal sum $T(f, \Lambda)(x) \in \mathrm{GrF}(x)$ (Definition 15.1.7, §XV.8), every chart $\varphi$ of $X$ around $x$ and every presentation $T$ of the partial quadrant $C$ of its model ($C = T^{-1}([0, \infty)^k \times V)$), the restriction of $L : T_xX \to W_x$ to the reduced tangent space $T^R_xX$ is surjective. $T^R_xX$ is Part II's reduced tangent fibre (Definition 2.5.8), read in the chart $\varphi$ and the presentation $T$ ⟦Chapter2/ReducedTangentIntrinsic · MPolyfold.reducedTangentFiber⟧.

*Readings.* (a) The text quantifies over $x \in \operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)$; the formal definition takes $x$ in $X_1$, where $T(f, \Lambda)$ is defined. Nothing is lost, because the support lies in $X_1$ (below). (b) The text writes $T(f, \Lambda)(x) = \sum_i\sigma_iL_i$ with $\sigma_i > 0$; these $L_i$ are the elements of the support of the formal sum, the operators with positive coefficient. (c) "$T^R_xX$ (Definition 2.5.8)" is read in every chart and every presentation; since $T^R_xX$ does not depend on them, one suffices (below). (d) The text works over a tame ep-groupoid; tameness is not part of the formal definition, and it enters the statements that need $T^R_xX$ to be the right space, through Part I's results on tame M-polyfolds.

*The support lies in $X_1$* ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.mem_levelSet_one_of_pos⟧. *Statement.* For an sc-Fredholm section functor $f$ and an sc⁺-multisection $\Lambda$, $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$ implies $x \in X_1$. *Proof.* Take a local section structure $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ of $\Lambda$ on an open $O \ni x$ (the 2021 form, Part XI). The count formula gives $\Lambda(f(x)) = \sum_{i : f(x) = s_i(x)}\sigma_i$; the sum is positive, so some $i$ has $f(x) = s_i(x)$. The value $s_i(x)$ of an sc⁺-section lies in $W_{0,1}$, and an sc-Fredholm section functor is regularizing: $x \in X_0$ and $f(x) \in W_{0,1}$ give $x \in X_1$ ⟦Chapter12/CompactnessEp · EpStrongBundle.remark_12_1_1_regularizing_obj⟧ (the remark after Definition 12.1.1, Part XII).

*One chart suffices.* Every level-$1$ point lies in a chart with a presentation of its partial quadrant ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.exists_chart_presentation⟧. The reduced tangent fibre does not depend on the chart and the presentation (Proposition 2.4.10, Part II) ⟦Chapter2/ReducedTangentIntrinsic · MPolyfold.reducedTangentFiber_eq⟧, so surjectivity on it, checked in one chart and presentation at each point, holds in all ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_iff_exists⟧: given the check in $(\varphi, T)$ and another pair $(\psi, T')$, rewrite the reduced fibre of $(\psi, T')$ as that of $(\varphi, T)$.

*The reduced linearization.* The text calls the restriction $T^R_{(f,\Lambda)}(x) = \sum_i\sigma_iL_i|T^R_xX$ the reduced linearization. Formally it is the push-forward of the formal sum along $L \mapsto L|T^R_xX$, in a chart and presentation ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.reducedLinearization⟧. Two distinct operators may have the same restriction; their coefficients then add. With coefficients in $\mathbb Q^+$ nothing cancels, so the support of a push-forward is the image of the support ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · Finsupp.support_mapDomain_nnrat⟧: for $b = g(a)$ with $a$ in the support, the coefficient of $b$ is a sum of non-negative terms one of which is positive. Hence general position is the surjectivity of every operator of the reduced linearization ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_iff_reducedLinearization⟧.

*Witness* ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_witness⟧: over the trivial ep-groupoid of $\mathbb R^n$ with the bundle $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0 \to \mathbb R^n$, the zero section and the zero multisection (the multisection of the zero section, value $1$ at zero vectors) are in general position, and $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)$ is all of $\mathbb R^n$. The fibres are $0$, so every operator onto them is surjective. The witness shows that the all-charts form is satisfiable with nonempty support; it has zero fibres and is **weak** as a test of the condition.

**Remark 15.3.6, first sentence** (same) ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPosition.surjective⟧. *Statement.* In general position, every operator of the support of $T(f, \Lambda)(x)$, $x \in \operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f) \cap X_1$, is surjective. *Proof.* Choose a chart and a presentation at $x$; for $w \in W_x$ general position gives $\alpha \in T^R_xX$ with $L\alpha = w$, and $\alpha \in T_xX$. "Onto" is read operator by operator, as in Definition 15.2.1 (§XV.12); so general position implies transversality, which the proof of Theorem 15.3.7 uses (§XV.22).

**Remark 15.3.6, second sentence** (narrower; stronger in its range) ⟦Chapter15/GoodPositionBranches · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_6_goodPosition_hyp⟧. *Statement.* Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section functor with Part I's tame germ data, $\Lambda$ an sc⁺-multisection, $(f, \Lambda)$ in general position and $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$. Then (a) $(f, \Lambda)$ is in good position at $x$ (Definition 15.2.4, §XV.15); (b) for every local section structure $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ of $\Lambda$ on an open $O \ni x$ and every $i$ with $s_i(x) = f(x)$, the section $(f - s_i)|_O$ of the restricted bundle $W|_O$ (§XV.10) is in general position at $x$ in Part I's sense (Definition 5.3.9). The text states this for $x \in \partial X$, with "good position, in fact even in general position"; (b) is the reading of "even in general position", Definition 15.3.5 being "a natural extension" of Definition 5.3.9. The formal statement holds at every point of the support, on the boundary or not: stronger in that respect.

*Proof.* Four steps, the first two in a chart.

*Step 1, the reduced surjectivity read in a chart* ⟦Chapter15/GoodPositionBranches · SBLocalization.exists_reduced_preimage⟧. Let $c$ be a strong bundle chart around $x$ with base chart $\varphi$, retraction $r$ on $U$, fibre retract $K$, and $z$ the level-$1$ point over $\varphi(x)$. Let $L$ be a chart operator $E_0 \to F_0$ whose intrinsic operator $\Phi_x^{-1} \circ L \circ T\varphi$ (§XV.7) is onto $W_x$ on $T^R_xX$. Then every $k$ with $(\varphi(x), k) \in K$ is $Lw$ for some $w$ in the chart's reduced tangent space at $z$ that also lies in the range of $Dr(z)$, the chart tangent space $T_zO$. The intrinsic operator is built from $L$ only when all chart vectors are mapped into the range of the fibre identification; in that case the preimage $\alpha \in T^R_xX$ of $\Phi_x^{-1}(k)$ gives $w$, the chart vector of $\alpha$, which lies in $T_zO$ and, by the chart description of the reduced fibre, in the reduced tangent space. Otherwise the intrinsic operator is $0$ by definition, surjectivity forces $W_x = 0$, and $w = 0$ serves.

*Step 2, the chart step* ⟦Chapter15/GoodPositionBranches · SBLocalization.isGeneralPositionGerm_subRestrict⟧. *Statement.* Let $s$ be sc⁺ on an open $O$, $f(x) = s(x)$, and $c$ a **tame** strong bundle chart of $W$ **centred** at $x$ ($\varphi(x) = 0$) with domain in $O$. If the intrinsic operator $(f - s)'(x)$ (§XV.7) is onto $W_x$ on $T^R_xX$, then $(f - s)|_O$ is in general position at $x$ in Part I's sense. *Proof.* Restrict $c$ to a chart $c'$ of $W|_O$, tame because $c$ is ⟦Chapter15/LocalIFT · SBLocalization.scDeriv_restrictTo_subRestrict⟧: in it the derivative at $0$ of the principal part of $(f - s)|_O$ composed with the retraction is the chart operator $D((\hat f - \hat s) \circ r)(0)$ of $W$. The section $(f - s)|_O$ is tame sc-Fredholm (§XV.10) and vanishes at $x$. By Step 1 the linearization $\mathrm{Lin}(0)$ of $(f - s)|_O$ in $c'$ maps the reduced tangent space onto the fibre $K_0$, in particular it is onto. Next, $\ker\mathrm{Lin}(0) + T^R_0 = T_0O$: for $v \in T_0O$, $k = \mathrm{Lin}(0)v$ lies in the fibre of $K$ over $0$, because the derivative of the principal part composed with $r$ is fixed by the fibre projection $\rho$ at a zero; Step 1 gives a reduced $w$ with $\mathrm{Lin}(0)w = k$; and $v = (v - w) + w$ with $v - w \in \ker\mathrm{Lin}(0)$. These two facts are Part I's chart criterion for general position, proved in Part I's chapter on general position ⟦Chapter5/CylinderPerturbation · isGeneralPositionGerm_of_sup_eq⟧, which concludes.

*Step 3, every branch through $f(x)$* ⟦Chapter15/GoodPositionBranches · SBLocalization.isGeneralPositionGerm_branch⟧. Let $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ be a local structure on $O \ni x$ and $s_i(x) = f(x)$. The point $x$ is smooth, being a zero of the regularizing section $f - s_i$ ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_smoothSet_of_eq⟧, in particular in $X_1$. The tame germ data of $f$ give a tame chart centred at $x$; restricted to $O$ it stays tame and centred with domain in $O$. With the local structure and this chart as local data (§XV.8), $T(f, \Lambda)(x)$ is their formal sum, so the intrinsic operator $(f - s_i)'(x)$ is in its support, the branch passing through $f(x)$ ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_support_linSumIntr_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/LinearizationIntrinsic · SBLocalization.linearization_eq⟧. General position makes it onto on $T^R_xX$ in this chart, and Step 2 applies.

*Step 4, good position.* Definition 15.2.4 asks three things at $x$. $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$ is the hypothesis. $T(f, \Lambda)(x)$ is onto: $x \in X_1$ by the lemma above, and every operator of the support is onto by the first sentence. For some local structure on an open $O \ni x$, given by the sc⁺-multisection, every branch through $f(x)$ is in Part I's good position (Definition 3.6.6): by Step 3 it is in general position, and general position implies good position for a tame sc-Fredholm section at a zero, Part I's Lemma 5.26 ⟦Chapter5/Transversality · StrongBundle.IsGeneralPositionGerm.isGoodPositionGermTame⟧, applied to $(f - s_i)|_O$, which is tame sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter14/LocalScPlus · SBLocalization.isScFredholmSectionTame_subRestrict⟧ and vanishes at $x$ ⟦Chapter14/LocalScPlus · SBLocalization.isZeroAt_subSec_iff⟧.

*Narrower* by the inherited hypothesis: Part I's tame germ data of $f$, the hypothesis of all of §15.2 (§§XV.13–XV.16), inherited from Part I's Theorems 3.1.18 and 3.6.8, without which Theorem 3.6.8 does not hold as we read it (Part III). Here it supplies the tame chart centred at $x$ of Step 2. Witness of all hypotheses together: the zero section of $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0 \to \mathbb R^n$ with the zero multisection, which has tame germ data ⟦Chapter15/InteriorPerturbationWitness · SBLocalization.theorem_15_2_2_hyp_witness⟧ and is in general position ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_epSEP_zero⟧; it has zero fibres (weak). No $d_X = 0$ is needed: unlike Remark 15.2.3 (§XV.14), the boundary points are reached through good position.

**Remark 15.3.6, last sentence** (narrower). The text concludes that $\Lambda \circ f$ is a tame branched ep⁺-subgroupoid. Three formal statements carry this.

*Local branching* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_6_localBranching_hyp⟧. *Statement.* Under the hypotheses of the second sentence, at every $y$ with $\Lambda(f(y)) > 0$ the function $z \mapsto \Lambda(f(z))$ has a local branching structure in the sense of Definition 9.1.2 (2)–(4) (Part IX). *Proof.* By the second sentence $(f, \Lambda)$ is in good position at $y$, so Theorem 15.2.5 (§XV.16) applies at $y$: it gives a natural representation $U(y)$, a local section structure $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ and a chart, such that each $M_i = \{z \in U(y) : f(z) = s_i(z)\}$ is a smooth submanifold properly embedded in $U(y)$, the count $\Lambda(f(z)) = \sum_{i : f(z) = s_i(z)}\sigma_i$ holds on $U(y)$, and only branches through $f(y)$ meet $f$ in $U(y)$. The branching structure is indexed by the branches through $f(y)$, with the sets $M_j$, which contain $y$, and the weights $\sigma_j > 0$. The count identity of Definition 9.1.2 is the count formula with the sum restricted to these branches: the terms of the other branches vanish on $U(y)$ by the last clause of Theorem 15.2.5.

*Branched* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_6_isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_hyp⟧. *Statement.* If moreover the orbit space $|\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)|$ is paracompact, $\Lambda \circ f$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of $X$ (Definition 9.1.2). *Proof.* Each clause of the definition: the values are non-negative rationals; $\Lambda \circ f$ is a functor, because $f$ is a section functor and $\Lambda$ is a functor, $\Lambda(f(t\varphi)) = \Lambda(\mu(\varphi, f(s\varphi))) = \Lambda(f(s\varphi))$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isFunctorial_comp⟧; the support consists of smooth points, the tame germ data making $f - s_i$ regularizing ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_smoothSet_of_pos⟧; the paracompactness of $|\operatorname{supp}|$ is the hypothesis; and the local branching structures are the previous statement. *The added hypothesis*, the paracompactness of the orbit space of the support, is Definition 9.1.2 (1), new in the 2021 edition, which the remark does not address; it is not derived from the text. It is automatic when $|\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)|$ is compact, the situation of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.21); witness for $\mathbb R^n$ ⟦Chapter9/Propositions · EpGroupoid.paracompactSpace_orbitSupp_trivial_rn⟧.

*Tame* ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_6_isTameBranched_hyp⟧. *Statement.* Under the same hypotheses $\Lambda \circ f$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid and tame (Definition 9.3.6). *Proof.* Good position holds at every point of the support (second sentence), and the global form of Theorem 15.2.5 (§XV.16) ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_2_5_isTameBranched_hyp⟧ concludes: at each support point Theorem 15.2.5 with "tame" gives a tame local branching structure, a natural representation with the properness property whose sheets $M_j$ carry a tame induced structure (Definition 2.5.18); forgetting the tameness gives the local branching structures of Definition 9.1.2, and the tame structures are Definition 9.3.6. Witness of the tame global form on $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0$, with support everything ⟦Chapter15/TameWitness · SBLocalization.remark_15_2_3_isTameBranched_hyp_witness⟧ (zero fibres, weak).

*Narrower* in all three only through the inherited tame germ data and, for the global forms, the paracompact support of Definition 9.1.2 (1). The text writes "tame branched ep⁺-groupoid", read as "ep⁺-subgroupoid". The compact form, under the hypotheses of Theorem 15.3.7, is its "in particular" (§XV.21), where compactness supplies the paracompactness.

**General position along local isomorphisms and equivalences** (extra) ⟦Chapter15/LinearizationLocalIso · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtLevel_localIso_iff⟧. *Statement.* Let $f = \Psi \circ f' \circ \phi$ on $U$ along a local strong bundle isomorphism $W|U \to W'|U'$ covering $\phi$, and $\Lambda = \Lambda' \circ \Psi$ over $U$. At $x \in U$ with $\phi(x) = x'$, the reduced linearization of $(f, \Lambda)$ is onto at $x$ if and only if that of $(f', \Lambda')$ is onto at $x'$. *Proof.* The tangent map of $\phi$ is an isomorphism $T_xX \simeq T_{x'}X'$ carrying $T^R_xX$ onto $T^R_{x'}X'$ ⟦Chapter15/LinearizationLocalIso · SBLocalization.tangentIso⟧; the branches of $\Lambda$ are those of $\Lambda'$ pulled back, so each operator of $T(f, \Lambda)(x)$ is conjugate to the corresponding one of $T(f', \Lambda')(x')$, and ontoness on the reduced tangent spaces read through the chart $c' \circ \Psi$ is ontoness read through $c'$ ⟦Chapter15/LinearizationLocalIso · SBLocalization.surjective_toIntr_transportChart_iff⟧. *Consequences.* General position is invariant under pull-back along a strong bundle equivalence ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionEquivalence · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_pullback_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionEquivalence · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtLevel_pullback_iff⟧ and along a diagram of two equivalences ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionEquivalence · SBLocalization.SDiagram.isInGeneralPosition_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionEquivalence · SBLocalization.SDiagram.isInGeneralPosition_pushMult_iff⟧. The same argument with $T_xX$ in place of $T^R_xX$ gives transversality (Definition 15.2.1): ontoness at level $1$ is invariant under local isomorphisms ⟦Chapter15/TransversalityEquivalence · SBLocalization.isOntoAtLevel_localIso_iff⟧, so transversality is invariant under equivalences ⟦Chapter15/TransversalityEquivalence · SBLocalization.isTransversal_pullback_iff⟧ and along diagrams with $\Phi^*F = \Psi^*F'$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalityEquivalence · SBLocalization.SDiagram.isTransversal_iff_of_pullback_eq⟧. The invariance under morphisms is the case of the local isomorphism of a morphism. The statement is not in Chapter 15; it makes general position and transversality notions on polyfolds (Part XVI). *Witness* ⟦Chapter16/CanonicalModelTransferWitness · StrongBundleLocalizationExamples.isTransversal_iff_ofHom_z3InvHom⟧: a diagram $W \leftarrow W \to W$ over $\mathbb Z/3 \times \mathbb R \rightrightarrows \mathbb R$ whose right leg is not the identity; weak, since it acts as the identity on objects, so the two sides are the same statement.

### XV.21 Theorem 15.3.7 (2017: 15.4): the statement and its "in particular"

Theorem 15.3.7 (pp. 693–698) is the existence theorem for perturbations in general position. Over a tame ep-groupoid with sc-smooth bump functions, it takes an sc-Fredholm section functor $f$ with compact solution set $|S_f|$, a pair $(N, U)$ controlling compactness, and a continuous functor $h : X \to (0, 1]$. It produces an sc⁺-multisection functor $\Lambda$ with four properties, and then adds two claims: "in particular" $\Lambda \circ f$ is a compact, tame, branched ep⁺-subgroupoid, and $\Lambda$ "can be taken to be structurable". The formalization states the theorem as two propositions, one with and one without "structurable", and proves both. The proof is told in §§XV.22–XV.25; the final theorems are in §XV.26.

**Statement** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_existence_statement⟧. Suppose:
- $X$ is tame;
- $X$ admits sc-smooth bump functions, in the pointwise reading of Part XII, which Theorem 12.3.2 (1) shows equivalent to bump functions on the object M-polyfold ⟦Chapter12/BumpEp · EpGroupoid.theorem_12_3_2_1⟧;
- $f$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor with the tame germ data of §15.2, and $|S_f|$ is compact;
- $(N, U)$ controls compactness (Definition 15.3.1, §XV.17) ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.ControlsCompactness⟧. That is, $N$ is an auxiliary norm, $U$ is an open saturated set containing $S_f$, and the orbit set of $\{x \in U : N(f(x)) \le 1\}$ has compact closure in $|X|$;
- $h$ is continuous, takes the same value at the source and the target of every morphism, and satisfies $0 < h \le 1$.

Then there is an sc⁺-multisection $\Lambda$ with:
- (1) $N(\Lambda)(x) < h(x)$ for every $x$, where $N(\Lambda)(x)$ is the pointwise norm of Part XIII (Definition 13.2.3), the largest $N(w)$ over the $w \in W_x$ with $\Lambda(w) > 0$;
- (2) domain support in $U$;
- (3) $(f, \Lambda)$ transversal (Definition 15.2.1, §XV.12) ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.IsTransversal⟧;
- (4) $(f, \Lambda)$ in general position (Definition 15.3.5, §XV.20) ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPosition⟧.

*The structurable form* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement⟧ is the same proposition with $\Lambda$ structurable (Definition 13.3.6). The theorem with its addendum pairs the two: (1)–(4) under the hypotheses above, and the structurable form whenever $X$ has a good system ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7⟧.

*Readings.*
- The text's "$U$ … a saturated subset" is read as open and saturated, as in Definition 15.3.1.
- A "continuous functor $h : X \to (0, 1]$" is read as a continuous function on objects that is constant along morphisms. This is a functor into the trivial category on $(0, 1]$.
- (3) is "$T(f, \Lambda)$ is surjective on $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)$", i.e. every operator of the linearization of Definition 15.1.7 (§XV.8) is onto at every support point.
- (4) is formalized as the reduced linearization being onto at every support point. This follows the first sentence of the proof: "Point (4) is equivalent to" that requirement.
- "Structurable" is Definition 13.3.6.

*Explicit*: the only hypothesis not printed in the theorem is the tame germ data of $f$. It is inherited from §15.2 (§XV.16), where the boundary local perturbation needs it. The theorem itself uses no good system and no paracompactness of $|X|$.

**"In particular", compact and branched** (narrower) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_in_particular_hyp⟧. Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section functor with tame germ data, and let $(N, U)$ control compactness. Let $\Lambda$ be an sc⁺-multisection with $N(\Lambda) \le 1$, domain support in $U$, and $(f, \Lambda)$ in general position. Then $\Lambda \circ f$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid (Definition 9.1.2) with compact support (Definition 9.1.6 (2)).

*Proof.*
1. $f$ is continuous, being an sc⁰ section.
2. *The support is closed* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isClosed_supp_comp⟧. Near any point $x_0$, $\Lambda$ has a local representation $(s_i, \sigma_i)_{i \in I}$ on an open $O \ni x_0$, with positive weights. On $O$, $\Lambda(w) = \sum_{i : s_i(y) = w}\sigma_i$ for $w \in W_y$. Since the weights are positive, $\Lambda(f(y)) > 0$ if and only if $f(y) = s_i(y)$ for some $i$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.pos_iff_exists_eq⟧. So the complement of the support meets $O$ in $O \cap \bigcap_i\{y : f(y) \ne s_i(y)\}$. Each set $\{f \ne s_i\}$ is open in $O$, because two continuous sections into a Hausdorff bundle disagree on an open set. Hence the complement is open.
3. *Its orbit set is closed* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isClosed_orbitSupp_comp⟧. The support is saturated: $f(\operatorname{tgt}\varphi) = \mu(\varphi, f(\operatorname{src}\varphi))$, and $\Lambda$ is a functor, so $\Lambda \circ f$ is constant along morphisms. Hence the complement of $|\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)|$ is the image of the complement of the support under the orbit map. The orbit map is open (Proposition 7.1.8) ⟦Chapter7/Basic · EpGroupoid.proposition_7_1_8⟧, so this image is open.
4. *The support lies in the controlled set* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.supp_comp_subset_controlled⟧. Let $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$.
   - If $f(x) = 0$, then $x \in S_f \subseteq U$.
   - Otherwise $\Lambda$ is positive at a non-zero vector over $x$, so $x$ is in the domain support, hence in $U$.

   In both cases $f(x)$ lies in $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda)(x)$, so $N(f(x)) \le N(\Lambda)(x) \le 1$.
5. *Compactness* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isCompact_orbitSupp_comp⟧. $|\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)|$ is a closed subset of the compact closure that $(N, U)$ provides.
6. *Branched*. A compact space is paracompact. This gives the paracompact support that Definition 9.1.2 (1) asks for, and Remark 15.3.6 (§XV.20) supplies the local branching structures ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_6_isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_hyp⟧.

Only (1) (with $h \le 1$), (2) and (4) are used. (3) follows from (4) ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPosition.surjective⟧. *Narrower*: the tame germ data are inherited from §15.2, and here they enter the conclusion, through good position at every support point (§XV.16).

**"In particular", tame** (narrower) ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_in_particular_tame_hyp⟧. Under the same hypotheses, $\Lambda \circ f$ is moreover a tame branched ep⁺-subgroupoid (Definition 9.3.6).

*Proof.* The first two conclusions are the previous theorem. Tameness is the tame form of Remark 15.3.6 (§XV.20) ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · SBLocalization.remark_15_3_6_isTameBranched_hyp⟧, applied with the paracompactness that the compact support gives.

**The zero multisection.** Suppose $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is already in general position, where $\Lambda_0$ is the trivial multisection (weight $1$ at the zero vector). Then $\Lambda_0$ has all the conclusions of the structurable form, for every $h > 0$ and every $U$, provided $|X|$ is paracompact (needed for "structurable") ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_isInGeneralPosition_zero⟧:
- its pointwise norm is $0$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_zeroMultisection⟧;
- its domain support is empty ⟦Chapter14/NormControl · SBLocalization.domSupp_zeroMultisection⟧.

On the bundle $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0 \to \mathbb R^n$, every pair is in general position, because every operator onto the zero fibre is surjective ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_epSEP_zero⟧. So the structurable form holds there with $\Lambda = \Lambda_0$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero⟧. This instance shows that the statement is not vacuous. It is weak: the fibres are zero and there is no boundary.

### XV.22 Proof of Theorem 15.3.7, I: linear families, (15.1), and the assembly

The text's proof:
- builds a family $\Lambda_t$, $t \in \mathbb R^{\bar\ell}$, of multisections from local pieces at the points of $S_f$;
- shows that the parametrized problem is onto on reduced tangent spaces at $\{0\} \times S_f$ ((15.1));
- uses the compactness (15.2) to stay near $S_f$ for small $|t|$;
- picks a small regular value $t_0$ of the projection to the parameter space, by Sard's theorem on the intersections of faces.

The formalized proof of (1)–(4) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_existence⟧ follows this plan in five steps:
- (a) at every point of $S_f$, small sections with (15.1) near that point ("seeds"; §§XV.23–XV.24);
- (b) from the seeds, a family good on a saturated neighbourhood of each point of $S_f$ (§XV.23);
- (c) by compactness of $|S_f|$, one family good on a neighbourhood of $S_f$ (this subsection);
- (d) for this family, general position for almost every small $t$ (§XV.25);
- (e) the choice of $t$ (this subsection).

The structurable form ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_goodSystem⟧ has the same five steps with structurable families. This subsection gives the objects the proof works with and steps (c) and (e).

**Linear combinations of sections** ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.paramSec⟧. For sections $s_1, \dots, s_\ell$ and $t \in \mathbb R^\ell$, the section $\sum_jt_js_j$ is taken fibrewise, in the linear structure of $W_y$:
- it is a section, and an sc⁺-section when each $s_j$ is one;
- concatenating the parameters adds the combinations: $\sum_j t_js_j + \sum_k t'_ks'_k$ is the combination of the concatenated sections at the concatenated parameter ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.paramSec_append⟧;
- $N(\sum_jt_js_j(y)) \le \sum_j|t_j|\,N(s_j(y))$, by subadditivity and homogeneity of the auxiliary norm on the fibres ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.normW_paramSec_le⟧.

**Linear families** ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.IsLinearFamily⟧. A family $t \mapsto \Lambda_t$ of multisections, $t \in \mathbb R^\ell$, is *linear* if around every point $x_0$ there are:
- an open $O \ni x_0$;
- sections $s_{ij}$ on $O$ ($i \in I$ finite, $j \le \ell$);
- weights $\sigma_i > 0$ with $\sum_i\sigma_i = 1$;

such that, for every $t$, the sections $\sum_jt_js_{ij}$ with the weights $\sigma_i$ form a local representation of $\Lambda_t$ on $O$. So one domain, one set of sections and one set of weights serve all $t$. This is how the text's $\Lambda_t$ and $\tilde\Lambda(t, w) = \Lambda_t(w)$ are formalized. The text asks that $\tilde\Lambda$ be an sc⁺-multisection of the pull-back of $W$ to $\mathbb R^{\bar\ell} \times X$; a linear family gives that representation directly. At $t = 0$ every branch is the zero section, so $\Lambda_0$ is the trivial multisection.

The pointwise norm of a linear family is controlled by its sections:
- the norm $N(\Lambda)(x)$ is attained at a branch value of any local representation ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_le_of_isLocalRepOn⟧;
- so if $N(s_{ij}) \le b$ then $N(\Lambda_t)(x) \le |t|_1\,b$, where $|t|_1 = \sum_j|t_j|$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_le_of_linear⟧.

**(15.1) at a point** ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAt⟧. Let $x$ be a level-$1$ point. The family satisfies (15.1) at $x$ if it has a linear representation around $x$ with the following property. For every operator $L$ of the linearization $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$ (§XV.8), every chart presentation of the reduced tangent space $T^R_xX$, every branch $i$ and every $w \in W_x$, there are $v \in T^R_xX$ and $\tau \in \mathbb R^\ell$ with
$$Lv + \textstyle\sum_j\tau_js_{ij}(x) = w.$$

The text writes (15.1) as "$T^R_{(\tilde f, \tilde\Lambda)}(0, x)$ is onto". Its local constructions phrase it as: the image of $f'(x)$ on $T^R_xX$, together with the vectors $s_{ij}(x)$, spans $W_x$. Two readings are made:
- $f'(x)$ is read as the operator of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$, which at a zero of $f$ is the linearization of $f$ (§XV.24);
- the $t$-derivative of $f - \sum_jt_js_{ij}$ is $\tau \mapsto -\sum_j\tau_js_{ij}(x)$, and its sign is immaterial for surjectivity.

At points with $d_X(x) = 0$, $T^R_xX = T_xX$, and (15.1) is the text's interior condition. So the interior and boundary constructions of the text become one construction.

**Sums of families** ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.famAdd⟧. For families $\Lambda$ over $\mathbb R^\ell$ and $\Lambda'$ over $\mathbb R^{\ell'}$, the sum over $\mathbb R^{\ell+\ell'}$ is $(\Lambda \oplus \Lambda')_{(t, t')} = \Lambda_t \oplus \Lambda'_{t'}$, the sum of multisections of Definition 13.2.9. This is the text's $\Lambda_t = \bigoplus_i\Lambda^{x_i}_{t_i}$.
- *Local representations of a sum* ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.isLocalRepOn_famAdd⟧. If $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ represents $\Lambda$ and $(s'_{i'}, \sigma'_{i'})$ represents $\Lambda'$ on $O$, then the sections $s_i + s'_{i'}$ with weights $\sigma_i\sigma'_{i'}$ represent the sum. The pairs $(i, i')$ are re-enumerated as one finite index set; the weights sum to $1$; the sums are sc⁺ on $O$. The value of the sum at $w$ is $\sum\{\sigma_i\sigma'_{i'} : s_i(y) + s'_{i'}(y) = w\}$, which is how Definition 13.2.9 computes a sum on a common representation domain.
- *Linearity* ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.IsLinearFamily.famAdd⟧. With linear representations, the branches $\sum_jt_js_{ij} + \sum_kt'_ks'_{i'k}$ are the combinations of the concatenated sections (`paramSec_append` above). So the sum of linear families is linear, on $O \cap O'$.
- *(15.1) for a sum* ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAt.famAdd_left⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAt.famAdd_right⟧. (15.1) for either summand at $x$ gives it for the sum. At $t = 0$ the sum is $\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_0 = \Lambda_0$, so the operators $L$ are the same. For a branch $(i, i')$ of the sum, solve with the summand that has (15.1), and put $\tau = 0$ on the parameters of the other summand.
- *Two inequalities the text uses silently.*
  - $N(\Lambda \oplus \Lambda')(x) \le N(\Lambda)(x) + N(\Lambda')(x)$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_multisectionAdd_le⟧. A vector in the support of the sum is $a + b$ with $\Lambda(a) > 0$ and $\Lambda'(b) > 0$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.exists_of_multisectionAdd_pos⟧, and $N(a + b) \le N(a) + N(b)$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.normW_add_le⟧.
  - The domain support of a sum lies in the union of the domain supports ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.domSupp_multisectionAdd_subset⟧. Off both, each summand is trivial, and so is the sum; the domain support is a closure, and the closure of a union of two sets is the union of their closures.

**Families good on a set** ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.ScPlusGPFamily⟧. For a set $Q$, a family *good on $Q$* is a linear family $\Lambda$ over some $\mathbb R^\ell$ with:
- every $\Lambda_t$ an sc⁺-multisection;
- $N(\Lambda_t)(x) \le |t|_1\,h(x)$ for all $t$ and $x$;
- domain supports in $U$;
- (15.1) at every level-$1$ point of $S_f \cap Q$.

The structurable twin ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.GPFamily⟧ asks every $\Lambda_t$ to be structurable instead.

The operations on good families:
- A family good on $Q$ is good on every subset of $Q$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.GPFamily.mono⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.ScPlusGPFamily.mono⟧.
- *The empty family* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.ScPlusGPFamily.zero⟧ has $\ell = 0$ and $\Lambda_t = \Lambda_0$, and is good on $\emptyset$. It is linear with one branch, the zero section of weight $1$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.isLocalRepOn_zeroMultisection⟧.
  - The structurable empty family ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.GPFamily.zero⟧ needs $\Lambda_0$ to be structurable. With a good system it is, with the zero decoration ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.isStructurable_zeroMultisection_of_goodSystem⟧.
- *Sums* ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.ScPlusGPFamily.add⟧. Families good on $Q$ and $Q'$ add to a family good on $Q \cup Q'$:
  - the sum of sc⁺-multisections is one (Definition 13.2.9) ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionAlgebra · SBLocalization.definition_13_2_9⟧;
  - the norm bound adds, $|t|_1h + |t'|_1h = |(t, t')|_1h$;
  - the domain supports stay in $U$;
  - (15.1) holds on $Q$ by `famAdd_left` and on $Q'$ by `famAdd_right`.

  The structurable sum ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.GPFamily.add⟧ is the same, with Proposition 13.3.12 for the structurability of a sum ⟦Chapter13/StructurableMultisection · SBLocalization.proposition_13_3_12⟧.
- By induction on a finite index set, with the empty family as the empty sum, families good on $Q_1, \dots, Q_k$ give a family good on $\bigcup_iQ_i$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.nonempty_scPlusGPFamily_biUnion⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.nonempty_gpFamily_biUnion⟧.
- So without a good system, sums of sc⁺-families need nothing; with structurable families, the empty sum needs a good system. This is the only place where the two forms of the theorem differ.

**Step (c), from local to global** ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.exists_scPlusGPFamily_of_compact⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.exists_gpFamily_of_compact⟧. Suppose every $x \in S_f$ lies in an open saturated $Q_x$ carrying a good family. In the structurable form this hypothesis is named ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.HasLocalGPFamilies⟧. Then there is one family good on an open $Q \supseteq S_f$.
- The sets $\pi(Q_x)$ are open in $|X|$, since $\pi$ is open (Proposition 7.1.8), and they cover the compact $|S_f|$. Choose finitely many $x_1, \dots, x_k$ and put $Q = \bigcup_iQ_{x_i}$.
- $S_f \subseteq Q$: a point of $S_f$ has its orbit in some $\pi(Q_{x_i})$, and $Q_{x_i}$ is saturated.
- The family on $Q$ is the sum of the $k$ families.

The sc⁺ version first saturates the open neighbourhoods it is given (§XV.23). This is the text's "From local to global", with $\bar\ell = \ell_{x_1} + \dots + \ell_{x_k}$.

**Steps (d) and (e), the choice of $t$.** Step (d) (§XV.25) gives $\varepsilon > 0$ and a Lebesgue-null set off which $(f, \Lambda_t)$ is in general position for $\|t\| < \varepsilon$ (sup norm on $\mathbb R^\ell$). The hypothesis $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1$ it needs follows from $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1h$ and $h \le 1$; (15.1) holds on $S_f$ because $S_f \subseteq Q$.
- Put $r = \min(\varepsilon, 1/(\ell + 1)) > 0$. A ball of positive radius has positive measure, so it is not contained in a null set. Hence there is $t$ with $\|t\| < r$ and $(f, \Lambda_t)$ in general position ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.exists_norm_lt_of_ae⟧.
- From $\|t\| < 1/(\ell + 1)$: $|t|_1 \le \ell\|t\| < 1$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.sum_abs_lt_one⟧.
- (1): $N(\Lambda_t)(x) \le |t|_1h(x) < h(x)$, since $h(x) > 0$.
- (2): the family's domain supports lie in $U$.
- (4) is the choice of $t$.
- (3) follows from (4) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.isTransversal_of_isInGeneralPosition⟧. A support point is a level-$1$ point, and an operator that is onto on the reduced tangent space $T^R_xX \subseteq T_xX$ is onto.

*Readings.*
- The text's $\varepsilon_0$, "so that $N(\Lambda_t)(x) < 1$ … provided $|t| \le \varepsilon_0$", is $1/(\ell + 1)$ in the sup norm. The bound actually used is $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1h$, which gives (1) with $h$ and not only with $1$.
- "A set of measure zero … for $t$ in its complement" is "for almost every $t$".
- The text's remark that the complement "contains points arbitrarily close to $0$" is the positive measure of balls.

*Departures from the text's assembly.*
- The text starts from "a large parameter space $\mathbb R^h$, i.e. $h >> 0$". In the formalization the number of parameters is whatever the construction produces, the sum $\ell_{x_1} + \dots + \ell_{x_k}$ of the local ones.
- The text views $\tilde\Lambda$ as an sc⁺-multisection over $\mathbb R^{\bar\ell} \times X$ and $\tilde f(t, x) = (t, f(x))$ as an sc-Fredholm section there. The formalization never forms this pair on the product ep-groupoid. Everything it needs from $\tilde\Lambda$ is in the linear representations: (15.1) at the points of $S_f$, the norm bound, and the domain supports. The product appears only branch by branch, on $\mathbb R^\ell \times O$ with $O$ open in the object M-polyfold (§XV.25), where Part I applies.

**The assembly with the two analytic steps as hypotheses** ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_hyp⟧. Steps (c) and (e) for structurable families, as one statement. Its hypotheses:
- a good system;
- compact $|S_f|$;
- $(N, U)$ controlling compactness;
- $0 < h \le 1$;
- the local families of step (b) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.HasLocalGPFamilies⟧;
- the parametric general position of step (d) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.HasParametricGeneralPosition⟧. This is stated for every linear family of sc⁺-multisections with $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1$, domain supports in $U$ and (15.1) on $S_f$.

Its conclusion is a structurable $\Lambda$ with (1)–(4). The structurable form of the theorem then follows from a good system and the two steps ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_hyp⟧. Both steps are proved from the theorem's hypotheses in §§XV.23–XV.25, so these statements are the skeleton of the final proof, not additional assumptions of it.

### XV.23 Proof of Theorem 15.3.7, II: the local constructions

The text's "Local constructions at $x \in S_f$":
- choose vectors $w_1, \dots, w_\ell \in W_x$ completing the image of $f'(x)$, on $T^R_xX$ at boundary points;
- extend them to sc⁺-sections $s^1_j$ supported in an invariant $V(x)$ with closure in $U(x)$;
- symmetrize over $G_x$;
- take the atomic multisection;
- invoke the implicit function theorem for a neighbourhood $Q(x)$ on which the parametrized problem stays onto.

The formalization follows this order. It needs one more tool, the transport of (15.1) along morphisms, because the symmetrized branches are moved by the isotropy group, and because the text passes to saturations "by equivariance".

**(15.1) with given vectors** ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoVec⟧. At a level-$1$ point $x$ and for vectors $v_1, \dots, v_\ell \in W_x$: for every operator $L$ of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$, every chart presentation of $T^R_xX$ and every $w \in W_x$, there are $u \in T^R_xX$ and $\tau$ with $Lu + \sum_j\tau_jv_j = w$. (15.1) for a family at $x$ is this property for the values $s_{ij}(x)$ of every branch of a linear representation ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.isParamOntoAt_iff_vec⟧.

**Transport along morphisms** ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoVec.of_mor⟧. If $\varphi : x \to y$ and (15.1) holds at $x$ with $v_j$, it holds at $y$ with $\mu(\varphi, v_j)$.

*Proof.*
1. *The operators at $y$.* Every operator $L'$ of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(y)$ is the conjugate $\mu(\varphi, \cdot) \circ L \circ (T\varphi)^{-1}$ of an operator $L$ of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$. This is the functor property of the linearization (§XV.9) ⟦Chapter15/LinearizationFunctor · SBLocalization.theorem_15_1_1_functor⟧, read on the support of the pushed-forward formal sum.
2. *The reduced tangent space at $y$.* Take a chart at $x$, restricted to the sheet of $\varphi$, and transport it along $\varphi$. Then $T\varphi$ maps $T^R_xX$ into the reduced tangent space at $y$ computed in the transported chart ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.tangentMorCLE_mem_reducedTangentFiber_transport⟧. By chart independence of $T^R$ (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/ReducedTangentIntrinsic · MPolyfold.reducedTangentFiber_eq⟧, this is the reduced tangent space of every chart presentation at $y$.
3. *Solving.* $\mu(\varphi, \cdot) : W_x \to W_y$ is a linear bijection with inverse $\mu(\varphi^{-1}, \cdot)$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.muLin_surjective⟧. Write $w = \mu(\varphi, w_0)$ and solve $Lu + \sum_j\tau_jv_j = w_0$ at $x$. Then $T\varphi\,u \in T^R_yX$ and
$$L'(T\varphi\,u) + \textstyle\sum_j\tau_j\mu(\varphi, v_j) = \mu(\varphi, Lu + \sum_j\tau_jv_j) = w.$$

*Linear representations are carried too* ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.exists_linearRep_of_mor⟧. Suppose $\Lambda_t$ is a functor for every $t$ and $(\sum_jt_js_{ij}, \sigma_i)$ represents it on $O \ni x$. Transport each section along the local diffeomorphism of $\varphi$; this gives sections on a neighbourhood of $y$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.transSec_eq_muLin⟧. Since $\mu$ is fibrewise linear, the transported combinations are the combinations of the transported sections ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.paramSec_transSec_eqOn⟧. They represent $\Lambda_t$ there because $\Lambda_t$ is a functor. So (15.1) for a family passes along morphisms in both directions ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAt.of_mor⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoTransport · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAt.of_mor_inv⟧.

**Saturation** ("the same assertion also holds for points … in the saturation of $Q(x)$").
- $S_f$ is saturated: if $\varphi : a \to z$ and $f(a) = 0$, then $f(z) = \mu(\varphi, 0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.mem_solutionSet_tgt⟧.
- A family good on an open $V$ is good on the saturation of $V$ ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.GPFamily.saturate⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.ScPlusGPFamily.saturate⟧. For $z \in S_f$ in the saturation there are $a \in V$ and $\varphi : a \to z$. Then $a \in S_f$, so (15.1) holds at $a$ and passes to $z$ along $\varphi$.
- The saturation of an open set is open, because $\pi$ is open. So it suffices to produce, at each $x \in S_f$, an open $V \ni x$ carrying a good family ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.hasLocalGPFamilies_of_nhds⟧.

**Seed data** ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData⟧. Seed data consist of:
- a point $x$;
- a natural representation of $G_x$ on an open $U(x)$ with the properness property;
- an open $V(x) \ni x$ invariant under the action, with $\operatorname{cl}V(x) \subseteq U(x)$;
- sc⁺-sections $s_1, \dots, s_\ell$ of $W$ supported in $V(x)$.

Such $U(x)$, $V(x)$ exist inside any open neighbourhood of $x$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · SBLocalization.exists_atomicNbhd⟧ (Theorem 7.1.13 and the proof of Theorem 7.5.7).

**The atomic family** ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.Λ⟧. For each $t$, $\Lambda_t$ is Part XIII's atomic multisection generated by the seed $\sum_jt_js_j$. Its branches on $U(x)$ are the translates
$$(\textstyle\sum_jt_js_j)^g(y) = \mu\big(\Gamma(g, g^{-1} * y), \sum_jt_js_j(g^{-1} * y)\big), \qquad g \in G_x,$$
which is the text's $s^{x,g}_t$. Each $\Lambda_t$ is an sc⁺-multisection.
- *Linear branches on $U(x)$* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.isLocalRepOn_branch⟧. Since $\mu$ is linear on fibres, $(\sum_jt_js_j)^g = \sum_jt_js_j^g$ ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.natTerm_paramSec⟧. So on $U(x)$ the family has the linear representation with sections $s^g_j$ and weights $1/|G_x|$.
- *Away from the seed* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.isLocalRepOn_zero⟧. Off the saturation of $\operatorname{cl}V(x)$, $\Lambda_t$ is the trivial multisection, represented by the zero section.
- *Linear everywhere* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.isLinearFamily⟧. There are two cases for a point $x_0$.
  - If $x_0$ is the target of a morphism $\varphi$ from a point of $U(x)$, the representation on $U(x)$ is carried along $\varphi$ (`exists_linearRep_of_mor`).
  - Otherwise $x_0$ lies off the closed saturation of $\operatorname{cl}V(x)$, and the zero representation serves.
- *Structurable* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.isStructurable_Λ⟧. Given a good system, every $\Lambda_t$ is structurable: an atomic multisection is structurable (Theorem 13.5.3) ⟦Chapter13/AtomicStructurable · SBLocalization.theorem_13_5_3_of_goodSystem⟧. This is the text's "for fixed $t$ it is structurable".
- *Domain support* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.domSupp_Λ_subset⟧. It lies in the saturation of $U(x)$.
- *Norm* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.norm_le⟧. If $N(s_j) \le c$ and $c \le h$ on the saturation of $U(x)$, then $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1h$.
  - At a point reached from $U(x)$, every branch value is $\mu(\Gamma, \sum_jt_js_j(\cdot))$. Its norm is at most $|t|_1c$, since $N$ is invariant under $\mu$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicConstruction · SBLocalization.SymmetricFamily.ofSeed_normW_le⟧ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicConstruction · SBLocalization.AtomicData.pointwiseNorm_mult_le⟧. And $c \le h$ there.
  - At other points the pointwise norm is $0$.
- *(15.1)* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.isParamOntoAt⟧. Let $W \ni x$ be open such that the vectors $s_j(y)$ satisfy (15.1) at every point $y$ of $S_f \cap W$. Put
$$Q_0 = \bigcap_{g \in G_x}\big(U(x) \cap \Phi_g^{-1}(W)\big).$$
  This set is open (a finite intersection) and contains $x$. At $y \in Q_0 \cap S_f$, the branch $g$ takes the values $\mu(m, s_j(\operatorname{src}m))$, where $m = \Gamma(g, \Phi_{g^{-1}}(y))$ is a morphism from $\Phi_{g^{-1}}(y) \in W$ to $y$. Its source lies in $S_f$, since $S_f$ is saturated, and in $W$. So (15.1) holds there with $s_j$, and `IsParamOntoVec.of_mor` carries it to $y$ for the branch $g$. This is the text's "the linearization … at $(0, x)$ is also surjective for every $g$", extended from $x$ to the points of $S_f$ near $x$.
- *Together* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.SeedData.gpFamily⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.SeedData.scPlusGPFamily⟧. The atomic family is good on $Q_0$: structurable given a good system, and sc⁺ without one.

**Local families from local seeds** ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.hasLocalGPFamilies_of_seeds⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.exists_scPlusGPFamily_nhds⟧. The analytic input is named *local seeds* ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.HasLocalParamSeeds⟧. It says: at every $x \in S_f$, for every open $O \ni x$ and every $\varepsilon > 0$, there are
- finitely many sc⁺-sections $s_j$ supported in $O$ with $N(s_j) \le \varepsilon$, and
- an open $W \ni x$ such that the $s_j(y)$ satisfy (15.1) at every level-$1$ point $y$ of $S_f \cap W$.

Given local seeds, every $x \in S_f$ has an open neighbourhood carrying a good family.

*Proof.*
1. Put $c = h(x)/2 > 0$ and $W_1 = U \cap \{y : h(y) > c\}$, an open neighbourhood of $x$.
2. Choose $U(x) \subseteq W_1$ and $V(x)$ as above, and seeds with $O = V(x)$ and $\varepsilon = c$.
3. *$c \le h$ on the saturation of $U(x)$.* A point $y$ there is isomorphic to some $a \in U(x)$, and $h(y) = h(a) > c$ because $h$ is constant along morphisms.
4. *The saturation of $U(x)$ lies in $U$*, because $U$ is saturated.
5. So the atomic family has $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1h$, domain support in $U$, and (15.1) on $Q_0$.

The text asks $N(s^1_j(y)) \le \min\{1, h(y)\}$ on $U(x)$. The formalization takes the constant bound $h(x)/2$ and shrinks $U(x)$ so that $h > h(x)/2$ on it. This gives the bound on the saturation directly.

With local seeds, the structurable form is proved in one statement ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_hyp_seeds⟧ ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_seeds⟧. These are intermediate steps of the final proof.

**Seeds from two facts at single points** ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.hasLocalParamSeeds_of⟧. Local seeds follow from bump functions, the smoothness of the points of $S_f$, and two facts:
- *small vectors* ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.HasPointwiseParamOnto⟧: at every level-$1$ point $x \in S_f$ and for every $\varepsilon > 0$, there are smooth vectors $w_j \in W_x$ with $N(w_j) < \varepsilon$ satisfying (15.1) at $x$;
- *openness* ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.HasParamOntoOpen⟧: if the values of sc⁺-sections $s_j$ satisfy (15.1) at $x \in S_f$, there is an open $W \ni x$ such that they satisfy it at every level-$1$ point of $S_f \cap W$.

*Proof.*
1. At $x \in S_f$, take the small vectors with $N(w_j) < \varepsilon/2$.
2. Part I's Lemma 5.3.3, on the object M-polyfold with its bump functions (Theorem 12.3.2 (1)), extends each smooth $w_j$ to an sc⁺-section $s_j$ with $s_j(x) = w_j$, support in $O$, and $N(s_j) \le \varepsilon$ ⟦Chapter5/BumpFunctions · lemma_5_3_3⟧.
3. Then (15.1) holds at $x$ with the $s_j(x)$, and openness gives $W$.

Points of $S_f$ are smooth by the regularity that the tame germ data provide ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_smoothSet_of_eq⟧. The structurable form from the two facts and parametric general position ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_pointwise⟧ is the next intermediate step.

The two facts are the text's choice of the $w_j$ ("we can pick the $w_i$ as small as we wish") and its appeal to the implicit function theorem for $Q(x)$. Both are proved from the theorem's hypotheses in §XV.24.

*Departures from the text's local constructions.*
- The text's two cases, $d_X(x) = 0$ and $d_X(x) \ge 1$, are one construction, because (15.1) is stated on $T^R_xX$.
- The text's "(Interior)" and "(Boundary)" conclusions assert that the parametrized solution set over $\{|t| < \varepsilon_x\} \times Q(x)$ is a tame branched ep⁺-subgroupoid. The formalization does not use this structure: what the assembly needs from $Q(x)$ is (15.1) at the points of $S_f \cap Q(x)$. The local structure of the parametrized solution set enters only in §XV.25, branch by branch, through Part I.

### XV.24 Proof of Theorem 15.3.7, III: (15.1) and Part I's conditions (i)–(ii)

The two facts of §XV.23 concern (15.1), stated on the ep-groupoid with reduced tangent spaces. Part I's proof of Theorem 5.3.10 works with conditions (i)–(ii) for a parametrized section in an M-polyfold chart, with the full tangent space. This subsection proves the two conditions equivalent at a zero of $f$, and then derives both facts from Part I.

**Part I's conditions** ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · StrongBundleChart.IsParamGP⟧. Take a strong bundle chart centred at $y$ (chart point $0$, retraction $r$, model fibre $K_0$) and a presentation $T$ of its partial quadrant, whose minimal subspace $E_0$ is the chart's reduced tangent space. Let $F(a, u) = \operatorname{Lin}_g(0)u + \sum_ia_i\hat t_i(0)$ be the linearization of $g + \sum_i\lambda_it_i$ at $(0, y)$. The conditions are:
- (i) $F$ maps $\mathbb R^m \times T_0O$ onto $K_0$;
- (ii) the kernel of $F$ is transversal to $\mathbb R^m \oplus T^R$: every $w \in T_0O$ is $u + e$ with $e \in E_0$ and $F(a, u) = 0$ for some $a$.

**The bridge** (extra) ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoBridge · SBLocalization.isParamOntoVec_iff_isParamGP⟧. Let $x$ be a level-$1$ zero of $f$, let a strong bundle chart of $W$ be centred at $x$ with a presentation $T$, and let $s_j$ be sc⁺-sections. Then (15.1) at $x$ with the vectors $s_j(x)$ holds if and only if Part I's (i)–(ii) hold in that chart for $f + \sum_jt_js_j$.

*Proof.* Both sides are read in the chart.
1. *The operator.* At a zero of $f$, the only operator of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$ is the intrinsic form of the chart linearization $D((\hat f - \hat 0) \circ r)(0)$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoBridge · SBLocalization.mem_support_linearization_zero_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoBridge · SBLocalization.linAt_principal_eq_subDeriv⟧. It is taken at the chart point of $x$, which is $0$ ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoBridge · SBLocalization.levelLift_eq_zero_of_centred⟧.
2. *(15.1) in the chart* ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoBridge · SBLocalization.onto_chart_iff⟧. (15.1) says that $(v, \tau) \mapsto D((\hat f - \hat 0) \circ r)(0)v + \sum_j\tau_j\hat s_j(0)$ maps $(T_0O \cap E_0) \times \mathbb R^\ell$ onto $K_0$. The fibre $W_x$ is $K_0$ read through the chart, and $T^R_xX$ is $T_0O \cap E_0$.
3. *(i)–(ii) are equivalent to this reading* ⟦Chapter15/ParamOntoBridge · SBLocalization.isParamGP_iff_onto_chart⟧. Write $L$ for the chart linearization and $\hat s_j$ for $\hat s_j(0)$.
   - *Forward.* Given $k \in K_0$, (i) gives $(a, u)$ with $Lu + \sum a_j\hat s_j = k$. Then (ii), applied to $u$, gives $(a', u')$ with $Lu' + \sum a'_j\hat s_j = 0$ and $u - u' \in E_0$. So $(a - a', u - u')$ hits $k$ with $u - u'$ reduced.
   - *Backward.* (i) is immediate. For (ii), take $w \in T_0O$. Then $Lw \in K_0$; solve $Lu + \sum\tau_j\hat s_j = Lw$ with $u$ reduced. The pair $(-\tau, w - u)$ is in the kernel, and $w - (w - u) = u \in E_0$.
4. *Every chart presentation.* (15.1) quantifies over all chart presentations, while the right side uses one. The two agree because the reduced tangent space does not depend on the chart (Part I) ⟦Chapter2/ReducedTangentIntrinsic · MPolyfold.reducedTangentFiber_eq⟧.

*Extra*: the text states (15.1) with $T^R_xX$, while Part I's proof of Theorem 5.3.10 uses (i)–(ii) with the full tangent space. The text presumably takes their equivalence for granted when it cites "the corresponding implicit function theorem".

Tame strong bundle charts centred at a point of $S_f$, with a presentation, exist by the tame germ data ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.exists_tameChart_centred⟧.

**Small vectors** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.hasPointwiseParamOnto⟧. With bump functions and the tame germ data, the small-vectors fact of §XV.23 holds.

*Proof.* Let $x \in S_f$ be a level-$1$ point and $\varepsilon > 0$.
1. Take a tame chart centred at $x$.
2. Part I's proof of Theorem 5.3.10 provides sc⁺-sections $s_j$ of the object M-polyfold bundle for which $f + \sum_jt_js_j$ satisfies (i)–(ii) in this chart. They can be chosen with norm at most $1$ for any given auxiliary norm ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · exists_sections_isParamGP⟧.
3. Apply this to the auxiliary norm $(2/\varepsilon)N$; a positive multiple of an auxiliary norm is one. This gives $N(s_j) \le \varepsilon/2 < \varepsilon$.
4. The bridge turns (i)–(ii) into (15.1) for the vectors $w_j = s_j(x)$.
5. The $w_j$ are smooth, because $x$ is smooth and sc⁺-sections map smooth points to smooth vectors.

*Explicit*: this is the text's choice of $w_1, \dots, w_\ell$ "as small as we wish", made in a chart.

**Openness** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.hasParamOntoOpen⟧. For tame $X$ and $f$ with tame germ data, the openness fact of §XV.23 holds.

*Proof.* Let sc⁺-sections $s_j$ satisfy (15.1) at $x \in S_f$.
1. The bridge, in a tame chart centred at $x$, gives (i)–(ii).
2. Part I turns (i)–(ii) into general position of $F(t, y) = f(y) + \sum_jt_js_j(y)$ on $\mathbb R^\ell \times X$ at $(0, x)$ ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · isGeneralPositionGerm_paramSection_of_isParamGP⟧.
3. $\mathbb R^\ell \times X$ is tame ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · prod_rn_isTame⟧ and $F$ is a tame sc-Fredholm section ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21_tame⟧. So general position at a zero spreads to the zeros in an open $W_0 \ni (0, x)$ ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · exists_isOpen_isGeneralPositionGerm⟧.
4. Put $W = \{y : (0, y) \in W_0\}$. At a point $y \in S_f \cap W$, $(0, y)$ is a zero of $F$ in general position.
5. In a tame chart centred at $y$, Part I recovers (i)–(ii) ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · isParamGP_of_isGeneralPositionGerm⟧, and the bridge at $y$ gives (15.1) there.

*Explicit*: this is the text's "from the local implicit function theorem we deduce", made precise as the openness of Part I's general position.

Both facts are needed only at points of $S_f$, which are smooth, so centred tame charts are available.

### XV.25 Proof of Theorem 15.3.7, IV: achieving global transversality

**Parametric general position** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.hasParametricGeneralPosition⟧. Suppose $X$ is tame, $f$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor with tame germ data, $|S_f|$ is compact, and $(N, U)$ controls compactness. Let $t \mapsto \Lambda_t$, $t \in \mathbb R^\ell$, be a family of sc⁺-multisections with:
- $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1$;
- domain supports in $U$;
- (15.1) at every level-$1$ point of $S_f$.

Then there is $\varepsilon > 0$ such that $(f, \Lambda_t)$ is in general position for almost every $t$ with $\|t\| < \varepsilon$. This is step (d) of §XV.22. The proof does not use that the family is linear everywhere (the linear representations inside (15.1) at the points of $S_f$ suffice), nor bump functions, nor $h$.

The text proves this in "Achieving global transversality":
- it uses Theorem 15.2.5 for an $\varepsilon$ below which the parametrized solution set is a tame branched ep⁺-subgroupoid with surjective reduced linearizations;
- around each of its points $(t_0, x_0)$ it writes the branches as manifolds with corners $M_i$;
- it applies Sard's theorem to $F \cap M_i \to \mathbb R^{\bar\ell}$ for every intersection $F$ of local faces;
- it covers the set (15.3) by countably many, or by compactness finitely many, such neighbourhoods.

The formalization keeps the plan but makes three changes.
- It centres the local neighbourhoods only at points $(0, x)$ with $x \in S_f$. The form of (15.2) proved below shows that for small $t$ the support of $\Lambda_t \circ f$ stays near $S_f$.
- It works branch by branch, where each branch is a section of a strong bundle over an open subset of the object M-polyfold, so that Part I's results apply directly.
- Part I's Theorem 4.3.1 in local form carries the faces. This replaces the text's "obvious branched ep⁺-groupoid version of Theorem 4.3.1".

**(i) Part I's Sard step, locally** (extra) ⟦Chapter15/LocalSlice · StrongBundle.exists_null_isGeneralPositionGerm_paramSlice_near⟧. Let $X$ be a tame M-polyfold, $g$ a tame sc-Fredholm section, and $t_1, \dots, t_m$ sc⁺-sections. Suppose $F(\lambda, y) = g(y) + \sum_i\lambda_it_i(y)$ is in general position at a zero $z_0$. Then there are an open $W \ni z_0$ in $\mathbb R^m \times X$ and a null set $C \subseteq \mathbb R^m$ such that, for $\lambda \notin C$, the slice $F(\lambda, \cdot)$ is in general position at its zeros $y$ with $(\lambda, y) \in W$.

*Proof.*
1. $F$ is a tame sc-Fredholm section on the tame $\mathbb R^m \times X$ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · theorem_3_1_21_tame⟧ ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · prod_rn_isTame⟧.
2. General position at $z_0$ gives an open $W_0$ on which the zero set $A$ of $F$ is in general position ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · exists_isOpen_isGeneralPositionGerm⟧.
3. *Faces.* Part I's Theorem 4.3.1 in local form gives a null set $C_1$ off which the fibres $\{y : (\lambda, y) \in A\}$ are submanifolds in general position to the faces ⟦Chapter4/Families · exists_local_family⟧.
4. *Surjectivity.* Part I's Sard step gives a null set $C_2$ off which the linearizations of the slice are onto at its zeros near $z_0$ ⟦Chapter5/Transversality · exists_null_isLinSurjAt_paramSlice_near⟧.
5. *Assembling general position of the slice.*
   - The slice is a tame sc-Fredholm section ⟦Chapter3/TameGermsAddG · theorem_3_1_18_tame⟧.
   - Near a zero, its zero set is the fibre piece of $A$, a submanifold ⟦Chapter15/LocalSlice · MPolyfold.IsGeneralPositionAt.exists_inter⟧.
   - Part I's characterization of general position by good position of a tame germ applies ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · isGoodPositionGermTame_of_isGeneralPositionAt⟧.
   - So the slice is in general position at every zero in $W$, with $C = C_1 \cup C_2$.

*Extra*: Part I's Theorem 5.3.10 is stated globally, under compactness ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · theorem_5_3_10_of_family⟧. The proof of Theorem 15.3.7 needs its Sard step near one point.

**(ii) A branch as a Part I section.** Let $(\sum_jt_js_{ij}, \sigma_i)$ be a linear representation of the family on $O$, and fix a branch $i$.
- *The parametrized section.* On the open subset $O$ of the object M-polyfold (tame, since an open subset of a tame M-polyfold is tame), take $g = (f - 0)|_O$, the section of $W|_O$ of §XV.10, and $t_j = (-s_{ij})|_O$ ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.negRestrict⟧.
- *Its slices are the branches* ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.paramSlice_branch_eq⟧. The slice at $t$ of $g + \sum_jt_j(-s_{ij})|_O$ is $(f - \sum_jt_js_{ij})|_O$. Its zeros are the points where $f$ meets branch $i$ of $\Lambda_t$.
- *(15.1) gives Part I's (i)–(ii)* ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.isParamGP_restrictTo_of_onto⟧. Take a chart of $W$ centred at $x$ with domain in $O$, transported to $W|_O$. The chart operator of $g$ is the chart linearization of $f$ ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.principal_eq_fiberLin⟧, and the principal part of $(-s)|_O$ is $-\hat s$ ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.principal_restrictTo_negRestrict⟧.
  - For (i): solve with (15.1) and change the sign of $\tau$.
  - For (ii): for $w$, solve $Lv + \sum_j\tau_j\hat s_{ij} = Lw$ with $v$ reduced. Then $(\tau, w - v)$ is in the kernel and $w - (w - v) = v \in E_0$.

  This is the argument of the bridge (§XV.24), in the form needed here.
- *General position at $(0, y)$* ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.isGeneralPositionGerm_branchParam⟧. Suppose (15.1) holds at $y \in S_f \cap O$.
  1. $y$ is smooth ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_smoothSet_of_eq⟧.
  2. The tame germ data give a tame chart centred at $y$, restricted into $O$.
  3. The operator of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(y)$ is obtained from a local linearization datum of $\Lambda_0$.
  4. Then (15.1) gives (i)–(ii) for the branch, and Part I gives general position of the parametrized branch at $(0, y)$ ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · isGeneralPositionGerm_paramSection_of_isParamGP⟧.

  The $s_{ij}$ are sc⁺ on $O$, as the branches of $\Lambda_{e_j}$ ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.isScPlusSectionOn_of_paramSec⟧.

**(iii) Local general position** ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.exists_local_paramGP⟧. Suppose $X$ is tame, $f$ has tame germ data, the $\Lambda_t$ are sc⁺, $x_0 \in S_f$ is a level-$1$ point, and (15.1) holds at $x_0$. Then there are an open $V \ni x_0$, a $\delta > 0$ and a null set $C$ such that $(f, \Lambda_t)$ is in general position at every $y \in V$ with $\Lambda_t(f(y)) > 0$, whenever $\|t\| < \delta$ and $t \notin C$.

*Proof.*
1. Take the linear representation on $O \ni x_0$ from (15.1).
2. For each branch $i$, (ii) and (i), applied on $\mathbb R^\ell \times O$, give an open $W_i \ni (0, x_0)$ and a null set $C_i$.
3. Shrink each $W_i$ to a product $u_i \times v_i$. Take $\delta$ with the $\delta$-ball inside every $u_i$, put $v' = \bigcap_iv_i$, let $V$ be its image in $X$, and put $C = \bigcup_iC_i$.
4. *General position at the support points in $V$.* Let $y \in V$ with $\Lambda_t(f(y)) > 0$. The operators of $T(f, \Lambda_t)(y)$ are the linearizations at $y$ of $f - \sum_jt_js_{ij}$ over the branches through $f(y)$ (Definition 15.1.7, §XV.8). For each such branch, $y$ is a zero of the slice at $t$, so the slice is in general position at $y$.
5. In a chart at $y$ restricted to $O$, this gives the reduced surjectivity of that operator (§XV.16) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionLocal · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtLevel_of_isGeneralPositionGerm⟧. Hence general position at $y$ in the sense of Definition 15.3.5 at one point ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionLocal · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPositionAt⟧.

**(iv) Invariance under morphisms** (extra) ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPositionAt_of_mor⟧. General position at a point passes along morphisms: if $\varphi : y \to x$ and $(f, \Lambda)$ is in general position at $y$, it is so at $x$. The proof is that of the transport of (15.1) in §XV.23 without the parameters ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtLevel_of_mor⟧:
- the operators at $x$ are conjugates of those at $y$ (§XV.9);
- $T\varphi$ maps reduced tangent vectors to reduced tangent vectors;
- $\mu(\varphi, \cdot)$ is onto.

*Extra*: the text uses this silently when it passes to saturations "by equivariance" ("the properties of $(\tilde f, \tilde\Lambda)$ at $(0, x')$ are the same as those of $(0, x)$").

**(v) The form of (15.2) the proof uses** (extra) ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.exists_pos_auxLe_subset⟧. Suppose $f$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor, $(N, U)$ controls compactness, and $V \supseteq S_f$ is open and saturated. Then there is $\varepsilon > 0$ with $\{x \in U : N(f(x)) \le \varepsilon\} \subseteq V$.

*Proof.* By contradiction.
1. Suppose that for every $n$ there is $x_n \in U \setminus V$ with $N(f(x_n)) \le 1/(n + 1)$. The orbits $\pi(x_n)$ lie in the compact closure that $(N, U)$ provides, so they have a cluster point $q = \pi(z)$.
2. The object space is metrizable, so $z$ has a decreasing neighbourhood basis $B_k$. Since $\pi$ is open and $q$ is a cluster point, there are $n_k \ge k$ and $y_k \in B_k$ with $\pi(y_k) = \pi(x_{n_k})$.
3. Because $U$ and $V$ are saturated, $y_k \in U \setminus V$.
4. $N(f(y_k)) \le 1/(k + 1)$, since $N \circ f$ is invariant along morphisms: $f(\operatorname{tgt}\varphi) = \mu(\varphi, f(\operatorname{src}\varphi))$, and $N$ is invariant under $\mu$.
5. So $y_k \to z$ and $N(f(y_k)) \to 0$. The third property of an auxiliary norm (Part I's Definition 5.1.1 (3)) gives $f(y_k) \to 0_z$.
6. Since $f$ is continuous, $f(z) = 0$. Then $z \in S_f \subseteq V$, and $V$ is open, so $y_k \in V$ for large $k$, a contradiction.

On the support of $\Lambda \circ f$, $N(f(x)) \le N(\Lambda)(x)$, and $x \in U$ once the domain support lies in $U$ ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.auxLe_of_pos⟧. This is the argument of step 4 of the "in particular" (§XV.21).

*Extra* in this form: the text states (15.2) as the compactness of $\{(t, x) : |t| \le \varepsilon_0, \Lambda_t(f(x)) > 0\}$. It uses that compactness to keep the parametrized solution set within finitely many local neighbourhoods. The formalization proves exactly that consequence.

**Proof of parametric general position.**
1. *Local neighbourhoods.* Every $z \in S_f$ is smooth, hence a level-$1$ point, and (15.1) holds there. So (iii) gives $V_z$, $\delta_z$, $C_z$.
2. *Finite cover.* The open sets $\pi(V_z)$ cover the compact $|S_f|$. Choose a finite set $T$ of such $z$, and let $V$ be the saturation of $\bigcup_{z \in T}V_z$. It is open, saturated, and contains $S_f$.
3. *Staying near $S_f$.* (v) gives $\varepsilon_0$ for this $V$. Choose $\varepsilon > 0$ so that $\|t\| < \varepsilon$ implies $\|t\| < \delta_z$ for $z \in T$ and $|t|_1 \le \varepsilon_0$ (recall $|t|_1 \le \ell\|t\|$).
4. *The null set* is $C = \bigcup_{z \in T}C_z$, a finite union.
5. *General position.* Let $\|t\| < \varepsilon$ and $t \notin C$. General position is checked point by point ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionLocal · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_iff_forall_at⟧. Let $\Lambda_t(f(x)) > 0$.
   - By `auxLe_of_pos` with $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1 \le \varepsilon_0$, $x \in U$ and $N(f(x)) \le \varepsilon_0$, so $x \in V$.
   - So there are $z \in T$, $y \in V_z$ and $\varphi : y \to x$.
   - $\Lambda_t(f(y)) = \Lambda_t(f(x)) > 0$, because $f$ and $\Lambda_t$ are functors.
   - So (iii) gives general position at $y$, and (iv) carries it to $x$.

*Explicit*: this is the step "Achieving global transversality", with four readings:
- Sard "on the intersections of faces" is read as Part I's local Theorem 4.3.1 with its Sard step, branch by branch on $\mathbb R^\ell \times O$;
- the passage to saturations is (iv);
- "using the compactness … a finite number of sets … suffices" is the finite cover of $|S_f|$ together with (v);
- the text's appeal to Theorem 15.2.5 for the structure of the parametrized solution set is replaced by Part I's results, through (ii) and (i).

With parametric general position, the structurable form follows from the local families alone ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_hyp_local⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_local⟧. These are intermediate steps of the final proof.

### XV.26 Theorem 15.3.7 with the text's hypotheses, the structurable addendum, and Remark 15.3.8

**Theorem 15.3.7, (1)–(4)** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_existence⟧. Under the hypotheses of §XV.21 there is an sc⁺-multisection $\Lambda$ with (1)–(4).

*Proof*, as the Lean proof goes.
1. $N$ is an auxiliary norm, being part of "$(N, U)$ controls compactness".
2. *Local seeds* (§XV.23) come from:
   - the bump functions;
   - the smoothness of the points of $S_f$;
   - the small-vectors fact (§XV.24);
   - the openness fact (§XV.24).
3. *Local families* (§XV.23). Each $x \in S_f$ has an open neighbourhood carrying a family of sc⁺-multisections good for $(N, U, h)$. This uses the continuity of $h$, its invariance along morphisms, and $h > 0$.
4. *One family* on a neighbourhood $Q$ of $S_f$, by saturation and the compactness of $|S_f|$ (§§XV.22–XV.23).
5. *Parametric general position* for this family (§XV.25), with the bound $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1h \le |t|_1$ (from $h \le 1$), domain supports in $U$, and (15.1) on $S_f \subseteq Q$. This gives $\varepsilon$.
6. *The choice of $t$* with $\|t\| < \min(\varepsilon, 1/(\ell + 1))$ (§XV.22). $\Lambda = \Lambda_t$ has (1)–(4).

*Explicit*: the hypotheses are the text's, plus the tame germ data inherited from §15.2.
- The tame germ data are used for the smoothness of the points of $S_f$, the tame charts of the bridge, and the branches as Part I sections.
- Tameness of $X$ is used in the openness fact and in the local Sard step.
- Bump functions are used for the seeds.
- No good system and no paracompactness of $|X|$ are used.

**The structurable addendum** (narrower) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_goodSystem⟧. If $X$ has a good system, the structurable form of §XV.21 holds. The proof has the same five steps, with structurable families (§XV.22): the atomic families are structurable by Theorem 13.5.3, sums by Proposition 13.3.12, and the empty family with the good system. The last step is the intermediate statement from the two facts at single points (§XV.23). For paracompact $|X|$, Theorem 13.1.8 gives a good system ⟦Chapter13/GoodSystemExistence · EpGroupoid.theorem_13_1_8⟧, so the structurable form holds ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_paracompact⟧.

*Narrower*: the good system is not in the text's hypotheses. It is necessary for this part, since a structurable multisection (Definition 13.3.6) carries a good system, so the conclusion implies the hypothesis. The text's proof opens with "We shall construct a structurable perturbation", and the step "for fixed $t$ it is structurable" is Theorem 13.5.3, which in the formalization needs a good system (Part XIII). The text presumably has in mind the paracompact situation that Remark 15.3.8 calls usual in applications.

**The theorem with its addendum** ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7⟧. (1)–(4) always, and $\Lambda$ structurable whenever a good system exists.

**The intermediate statements** are steps of these two proofs. Each one proves the structurable form from a hypothesis that another step of the proof discharges:
- `theorem_15_3_7_hyp` and `theorem_15_3_7_of_hyp` (local families and parametric general position; §XV.22);
- `theorem_15_3_7_hyp_seeds` and `theorem_15_3_7_of_seeds` (local seeds; §XV.23);
- `theorem_15_3_7_of_pointwise` (the two facts at single points; §XV.23);
- `theorem_15_3_7_hyp_local` and `theorem_15_3_7_of_local` (local families alone; §XV.25);
- the form from the two facts and a good system ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionPointwise · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_paramOnto⟧, which the structurable addendum specializes by proving the two facts.

Each has its hypotheses satisfied on $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0 \to \mathbb R^n$:
- a good system there, by Theorem 13.1.8 on $\mathbb R^n$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.nonempty_goodSystem_epSEP_zero⟧;
- local families with $\ell = 0$ for every $f$, $N$, $U$, $h$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.hasLocalGPFamilies_epSEP_zero⟧;
- parametric general position, since every pair is in general position there ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.hasParametricGeneralPosition_epSEP_zero⟧;
- local seeds ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.hasLocalParamSeeds_epSEP_zero⟧;
- small vectors with $\ell = 0$ ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.hasPointwiseParamOnto_epSEP_zero⟧ and openness ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.hasParamOntoOpen_epSEP_zero⟧, both resting on (15.1) being automatic with zero fibres ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.isParamOntoVec_epSEP_zero⟧.
