The conclusion holds there in each form:
- ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero_of_hyp⟧
- ⟦Chapter15/LocalFamilies · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero_of_seeds⟧
- ⟦Chapter15/LocalSeeds · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero_of_pointwise⟧
- ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero_of_local⟧
- ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionPointwise · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero_of_paramOnto⟧
- ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_statement_epSEP_zero_of_goodSystem⟧

These witnesses are weak: the fibres are zero and there is no boundary, so every pair is in general position. They show only that the intermediate statements are not vacuous. The final theorems need no such witness, since all their added hypotheses are discharged, except the inherited tame germ data and, for the addendum, the necessary good system.

**Remark 15.3.8** (nothing to formalize). The remark says the theorem does not assume a paracompact orbit space, only an auxiliary norm, and that constructing auxiliary norms usually needs paracompactness of $|X|$.
- In the formalization, (1)–(4) need neither paracompactness nor a good system. The auxiliary norm enters only through "$(N, U)$ controls compactness".
- The addendum "structurable" needs a good system. Paracompactness supplies one (Theorem 13.1.8), and so does any structurable multisection.

So the remark holds as read for (1)–(4). For the addendum, paracompactness, or at least a good system, does enter. The part of point G.162 that concerns Theorem 15.3.7 is closed.

### XV.27 Theorem 15.3.9 (2017: 15.5): the statement, the face condition, and charging

Theorem 15.3.9, the Transversal Extension Theorem (p. 698), extends a perturbation given over the boundary to a perturbation over all of $X$ that is transversal, without changing it over $\partial X$. It is the tool for the cobordism part of Corollary 15.3.10 (§XV.33), where the boundary data are two regular perturbations sitting at the ends of the cylinder $[0, 1] \times X$. Its proof (pp. 698–699) has five steps: (a) a cut-off functor $h_0$ between $N(\Lambda)$ and $h$; (b) Theorem 14.1.9, which extends $\Lambda$ to a structurable $\Lambda_0$ with $N(\Lambda_0) \le h_0$; (c) transversality of $(f, \Lambda_0)$ near $\partial X$, from the hypothesis on the faces; (d) the argument of Theorem 15.3.7 away from $\partial X$, with families $\Lambda_t$ whose domain support avoids $\partial X$; (e) $\tilde\Lambda = \Lambda_t \oplus \Lambda_0$ for a small regular $t$. The formalization follows these steps in this order. This subsection gives the definitions, the statement, its readings and its witnesses; step (a) is §XV.28, steps (b)–(e) are §XV.29.

**General position at one point.** For a level-$1$ point $x$, $(f, \Lambda)$ is *reducedly onto at $x$* if every operator $L$ in the support of $T(f, \Lambda)(x)$ (Definition 15.1.7, §XV.8) restricts to a surjection $T^R_xX \to W_x$, the reduced tangent space $T^R_xX$ being read in every chart of $X$ around $x$ and every presentation of the partial quadrant of its model ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.IsReducedOntoAtPt⟧. This is Definition 15.3.5 at one point: $(f, \Lambda)$ is in general position iff it is reducedly onto at every level-$1$ point of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)$, by definition ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_iff_forall_reducedOntoAtPt⟧ (it is the same predicate as the pointwise general position of §XV.16, ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionLocal · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPositionAt⟧, there with the level-$1$ membership as a separate clause). In general position $(f, \Lambda)$ is transversal: every operator of the support is onto on the larger space $T_xX \supseteq T^R_xX$, and the support points are at level $1$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.IsInGeneralPosition.isTransversal⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.mem_levelSet_one_of_pos⟧.

**The boundary data.** Chapter 14's objects over $\partial X$ are used as Part XIV formalizes them. An *sc⁺-multisection over $\partial X$* (Definition 14.1.3) is a function $\Lambda : W \to \mathbb Q^+$ of which only the values over $\partial X$ enter: it is a functor on $W|\partial X$, and every $x \in \partial X$ has a good neighbourhood $U(x) = V(x) \cap \partial X$ carrying a *symmetric sc⁺-section structure*, a finite index set $I_x$ with a $G_x$-action and sections $s_i$ that are sc⁺ on the faces (Definition 14.1.3 (1)), equivariant (2), and represent $\Lambda$ by $\Lambda(w) = |I_x|^{-1}\#\{i : s_i(P(w)) = w\}$ (3) ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryMultisection · SBLocalization.IsBoundaryScPlusMultisection⟧ ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryMultisection · SBLocalization.BoundarySymmetricStructure⟧. It is *structurable over $\partial X$* (Definition 14.1.7) if it carries in addition a decoration and a family of index correspondences ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryStructured · SBLocalization.IsBoundaryStructurable⟧. Its *boundary domain support* is the closure of the set of $x \in \partial X$ over which $\Lambda$ charges a non-zero vector ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryStructured · SBLocalization.boundaryDomSupp⟧. Finally, $\Lambda$ *charges $\partial X$* if over every $x \in \partial X$ some $w$ has $\Lambda(w) \ne 0$ ⟦Chapter14/EmptyIndex · SBLocalization.ChargesBoundary⟧. Definition 14.1.3 takes $I_x$ non-empty, as its $|I_x|^{-1}$ presumes, and so does the formal structure. With (3), a branch $s_i$ gives $\Lambda(s_i(y)) \ge |I_x|^{-1} > 0$, so every sc⁺-multisection over $\partial X$ charges $\partial X$ ⟦Chapter14/EmptyIndex · SBLocalization.chargesBoundary_of_isBoundaryScPlusMultisection⟧ ⟦Chapter14/EmptyIndex · SBLocalization.IsBoundaryStructurable.chargesBoundary⟧; conversely, at a charged point the formula (3) with $I_x = \emptyset$ would give $\Lambda = 0$ on the fibre ⟦Chapter14/EmptyIndex · SBLocalization.BoundarySymmetricStructure.nonempty_I⟧. Theorem 15.3.9 needs the charging (below).

**The face condition** ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.IsBoundaryFaceOnto⟧. The text asks that $T_{(f|\partial X, \Lambda)}(x)$ be "surjective with respect to intersections of local faces" at every smooth $x \in \partial X$. It is read as follows: for every sc⁺-multisection $\Lambda'$ on $W$ that agrees with $\Lambda$ over $\partial X$, and every smooth level-$1$ point $x \in \partial X$ with $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$, $(f, \Lambda')$ is reducedly onto at $x$. Two readings are in this. (i) The intersections of the tangent spaces of the local faces through $x$ form a family whose smallest member is $T^R_xX$ (Part I, Definition 2.5.8), and surjectivity on a subspace implies surjectivity on every larger one; so the condition for all intersections is the condition on $T^R_xX$, which is general position at $x$. (ii) $T(f|\partial X, \Lambda)$ is not defined by the text: $\Lambda$ lives only over $\partial X$, and its linearization needs local sections near $x$, not only on the faces. The restriction of $(f - s_i)'(x)$ to $T^R_xX$ sees only the values of $s_i$ on the faces through $x$, so it should not depend on how $\Lambda$ is extended; the formal condition is therefore asked for every sc⁺ extension $\Lambda'$. That it does not depend on the extension is proved next; in the proof the condition is used for exactly one extension, the $\Lambda_0$ of step (b) (§XV.29).

**One extension suffices** ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.isBoundaryFaceOnto_iff⟧. *Statement.* If $\Lambda'$ is an sc⁺-multisection on $W$ equal to $\Lambda$ over $\partial X$, the face condition holds iff $(f, \Lambda')$ is reducedly onto at the smooth level-$1$ points of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f) \cap \partial X$. *Proof.* At a smooth $x \in \partial X$ the operators of $T(f, \Lambda)(x)$, restricted to $T^R_xX$, depend only on $\Lambda$ over $\partial X$ near $x$ ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.exists_linearization_eqOn_reduced⟧: if $\Lambda_1$, $\Lambda_2$ agree over $\partial X \cap Q$ for an open $Q \ni x$, every operator of $T(f, \Lambda_2)(x)$ agrees on $T^R_xX$ with an operator of $T(f, \Lambda_1)(x)$. In a tame chart centred at $x$, the retraction maps the points of the minimal subspace $K$ (the model of $T^R_xX$) to points with the degeneracy index of $x$, so these lie over $\partial X$, where every branch of $\Lambda_2$ through a point is a branch of $\Lambda_1$. For a branch $s$ of $\Lambda_2$ through $f(x)$, the sets of $K$ near $x$ on which $s$ equals one of the finitely many branches of $\Lambda_1$ are closed and cover; a finite union of closed sets with empty interior has empty interior, so $x$ lies in the closure of the interior of one of them ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.exists_mem_closure_interior_of_finite_cover⟧, and on that interior $s$ and the branch agree. The level-$1$ vectors being dense in $K$, the two have the same value and the same derivative on $K$ at $x$ ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.exists_scDeriv_eq_on_of_cover⟧. So reduced ontoness at $x$ passes from $\Lambda_1$ to $\Lambda_2$ ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtPt_of_eqOn_boundary⟧ ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtPt_of_eq_boundary⟧, and the condition for one extension gives it for all. The theorem with the face condition checked on one extension is ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryExtensionIndependence · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_of_one_extension⟧.

**Statement** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_statement⟧ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9⟧. Let $(P : W \to X, \mu)$ be a strong bundle over a tame ep-groupoid with $|X|$ paracompact, whose object M-polyfold admits sc-smooth partitions of unity. Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section functor with Part I's tame germ data and compact $|S_f|$, and $(N, U)$ control compactness (Definition 15.3.1, §XV.17; $U$ open and saturated). Let $\Lambda$ be structurable over $\partial X$, with boundary domain support in $U$ and $N(\Lambda) \le 1$ on $\partial X$, and let $h : X \to (0, 1]$ be a continuous functor with $N(\Lambda)(x) < h(x)$ for $x \in \partial X$. Assume the face condition. Then there is a structurable sc⁺-multisection $\tilde\Lambda$ with

1. $\tilde\Lambda = \Lambda$ over $\partial X$;
2. domain support in $U$, $N(\tilde\Lambda) < h$ everywhere, and $\tilde\Lambda$ $(N, U)$-admissible (Definition 15.3.3, §XV.18);
3. $(f, \tilde\Lambda)$ transversal (Definition 15.2.1, §XV.12);

and $\tilde\Lambda \circ f$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid with compact support.

*Readings.* "$U$ a saturated subset" is read as open and saturated, which Definition 15.3.1 contains. "$\Lambda$ is $(U, N)$-admissible" over $\partial X$ is read as boundary domain support in $U$ and $N(\Lambda) \le 1$ on $\partial X$, the two parts of Definition 15.3.3 that make sense over $\partial X$. "Onto on $\operatorname{supp}(\tilde\Lambda \circ f)$" is transversality. The word "tame" of the "moreover" is not in the formal conclusion: the $\tilde\Lambda$ that the proof produces is in general position (§XV.29), and for such a $\tilde\Lambda$ the tame form of the "in particular" of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.21) gives a tame branched ep⁺-subgroupoid, but the statement as formalized exports only the text's (1)–(3) with "branched" and "compact".

*Explicit.* The one hypothesis not printed is the tame germ data of $f$, inherited from §15.2 and Part I's Theorem 3.6.8, as everywhere in this Part. Tame strong bundle charts, which step (a) uses (§XV.28), exist at every object of a strong bundle over a tame ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter10/TameBundleCharts · SEP.exists_isTameChart⟧ ⟦Chapter10/TameBundleCharts · StrongBundle.exists_isTameChart⟧. Charging of $\partial X$ is necessary for the conclusion: if a structurable $\tilde\Lambda$ agrees with $\Lambda$ over $\partial X$, then $\Lambda$ charges $\partial X$ ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.chargesBoundary_of_theorem_15_3_9⟧, because a local representation of $\tilde\Lambda$ at $x \in \partial X$ (Definition 13.2.1) has weights summing to $1$, so it has a branch $s_i$, and $\tilde\Lambda(s_i(x)) \ge \sigma_i > 0$ ⟦Chapter14/EmptyIndex · SBLocalization.chargesBoundary_of_structurable_eq⟧; with Definition 14.1.3's non-empty index sets it holds for every boundary datum (above). So the form with tame strong bundle charts and charging as hypotheses ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_charged_statement⟧ is equivalent to the theorem ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_charged_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_statement_of_charged⟧. Partitions of unity on the objects and the paracompactness of $|X|$ are the text's.

**Witnesses** ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_9_hypotheses_halfSEP⟧ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_9_halfSEP⟧. On the half-plane bundle over $H = [0, \infty) \times \mathbb R$ with fibre $\mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter14/HalfSpaceBundle · HalfSpaceBundle.halfSEP⟧, with $f$ the graph of $\varphi(a, y) = a^2 + y^2 - 1$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.halfDiscSection⟧, $\Lambda$ the multisection of the zero section and $h \equiv 1$, every hypothesis holds: $\partial X \ne \emptyset$, the fibres are non-zero, $(0, 1) \in \operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f) \cap \partial X$, $|S_f|$, a half circle, is compact ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.hasCompactSolutionSetEp_halfDisc⟧, and the face condition holds, checked on one extension ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.isBoundaryFaceOnto_halfDisc⟧: at $(0, \pm1)$ the operator $(\alpha, \eta) \mapsto \pm2\eta$ is onto on $T^R = \{0\} \times \mathbb R$. On the same bundle the face condition can fail: for $\psi(a, y) = (a - 1)^2 + y^2 - 1$, whose zero set touches $\partial H$ at $0$, the operator $(\alpha, \eta) \mapsto -2\alpha$ vanishes on $T^R_0H$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.not_isBoundaryFaceOnto_tangentDisc⟧. The first witness is weak in one respect: $\Lambda = 0$ over $\partial H$, so step (b) extends the zero datum. A non-zero boundary datum is the multisection $\Lambda_v$ of the constant section $v = -3/4$ ⟦Chapter15/SingleSectionMultisection · SBLocalization.sectionMulti⟧, structurable ⟦Chapter15/SingleSectionMultisection · SBLocalization.isStructurable_sectionMulti⟧, with the same $f$: the boundary domain support is $\partial H$, the face condition is checked at $(0, \pm1/2)$, where $\varphi = -3/4$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionBoundaryDatum · HalfSpaceBundle.isBoundaryFaceOnto_halfDisc_constMulti⟧, every hypothesis holds ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionBoundaryDatum · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_9_hypotheses_constDatum⟧, and the extension charges $(x, -3/4)$ at every $x \in \partial H$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionBoundaryDatum · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_9_constDatum⟧; weak in that the ep-groupoid is trivial and $U = X$.

**The shape of the proof** ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_of_rel⟧. The theorem is assembled in the text's order: step (a) the cut-off (§XV.28), step (b) Theorem 14.1.9, steps (c)–(d) the *relative statement* ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative_statement⟧: given a structurable $\Lambda_0$ with domain support in $U$, $N(\Lambda_0) < h$, and $(f, \Lambda_0)$ reducedly onto at the points of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f) \cap \partial X$, there is a structurable $\tilde\Lambda$ equal to $\Lambda_0$ over $\partial X$, with domain support in $U$, $N(\tilde\Lambda) < h$, transversal and in general position; and step (e) with the "moreover" ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_hyp⟧. The relative statement is proved ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative⟧ from the relative local families ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative_of_local⟧, which exist ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · SBLocalization.relative_local_families_holds⟧. §XV.28 proves step (a); §XV.29 retells steps (b)–(e) and the proof of the relative statement.

### XV.28 Proof of Theorem 15.3.9, step (a): the cut-off functor $h_0$

**Statement** (same) ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.lemma_15_3_9_cutoff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.lemma_15_3_9_cutoff_statement⟧. Step (a) holds: for $X$ tame with $|X|$ paracompact and sc-smooth partitions of unity, $\Lambda$ an sc⁺-multisection over $\partial X$, $N$ an auxiliary norm, $U$ open and saturated containing the boundary domain support of $\Lambda$, and $h > 0$ a continuous functor with $N(\Lambda) < h$ on $\partial X$, there is a continuous functor $h_0 \ge 0$ with $\operatorname{tsupp}h_0 \subset U$, $h_0 < h$ everywhere, and $N(\Lambda) < h_0$ on the boundary domain support of $\Lambda$. These are the hypotheses that Theorem 14.1.9 asks of its function $f$ with $\tilde U = U$, and the margin $h_0 < h$ is used at the end of the proof.

*A reading.* The text asks for $h_0$ with $N(\Lambda)(x) < h_0(x) < h(x)$ for every $x \in \partial X$ and $h_0$ supported in $U$. Off the boundary domain support, over $\partial X$, the support of $\Lambda$ is the zero vector and $N(\Lambda) = 0$; at a point of $\partial X$ outside $U$ the two requirements would ask $0 < h_0 = 0$. So the strict lower bound is presumably meant on the domain support, which is what Theorem 14.1.9 asks; the formal statement says so. The text's $h_0 \le 1$ is not needed, $h_0 < h \le 1$ holding anyway in the application.

**Proof.** Write $C$ for the boundary domain support of $\Lambda$. Four facts about $\Lambda$ come first.

*The support over a boundary point.* If $S$ is a symmetric structure of $\Lambda$ on $V(x)$ and $y \in V(x) \cap \partial X$, then $w$ lies in the support of $\Lambda$ over $y$ iff $w = s_i(y)$ for some $i$ ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.BoundarySymmetricStructure.mem_suppAt_iff⟧: by the representation formula (3), $\Lambda(w) > 0$ iff the count $\#\{i : s_i(y) = w\}$ is positive, the index set being non-empty as soon as it contains one $i$. In particular the support over $\partial X$ lies in $W_{0,1}$, each $s_i$ being sc⁺ on the faces ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.IsBoundaryScPlusMultisection.suppAt_subset_oneSet⟧, so the pointwise norm $N(\Lambda)(y) = \sup\{N(w) : \Lambda(w) > 0\}$ is the maximum of the finitely many $N(s_i(y))$.

*$N(\Lambda)$ is a functor on $\partial X$* ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_tgt_of_boundary⟧. For a morphism $\varphi$ with source in $\partial X$, $\mu(\varphi, \cdot)$ carries the support over $s(\varphi)$ into the support over $t(\varphi)$, since $\Lambda$ is a functor on $W|\partial X$, and it preserves $N$, since an auxiliary norm is invariant under $\mu$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicConstruction · SBLocalization.normW_μ⟧; so the set of norms over $s(\varphi)$ lies in that over $t(\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.normW_image_suppAt_subset⟧, and the inverse morphism, whose source again lies in the saturated set $\partial X$, gives the other inclusion.

*$C$ is saturated* ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.isSaturated_boundaryDomSupp_of_isBoundaryScPlusMultisection⟧. The set of $x \in \partial X$ over which $\Lambda$ charges a non-zero $w$ is saturated: $\mu(\varphi, w)$ is charged over $t(\varphi)$, and it is not zero, since $N(\mu(\varphi, w)) = N(w) > 0$, an auxiliary norm vanishing only at zero vectors. The closure of a saturated set is saturated. And $C \subset \partial X$, $\partial X$ being closed ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.boundaryDomSupp_subset_boundary⟧.

*$N(\Lambda)$ is upper semicontinuous along $\partial X$* ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.exists_nhds_pointwiseNorm_lt⟧: for $x \in \partial X$ and $c > N(\Lambda)(x)$ there is an open $O \ni x$ with $N(\Lambda) < c$ on $O \cap \partial X$. Take a symmetric structure on $V(x)$. Its sections $s_i$ are sc⁺ only on the faces; with tame strong bundle charts at every object and sc-smooth partitions of unity, each extends to an sc⁺-section $\bar s_i$ on the open set $V(x)$ with $\bar s_i = s_i$ on $V(x) \cap \partial X$ ⟦Chapter14/OpenExtension · SBLocalization.exists_scPlusSectionOn_extension_of_face⟧ (the extension lemma of Part XIV behind Proposition 14.1.4). The functions $y \mapsto N(\bar s_i(y))$ are continuous on $V(x)$. With $c' = (N(\Lambda)(x) + c)/2$, the set $O = V(x) \cap \bigcap_i\{N \circ \bar s_i < c'\}$ is open, contains $x$ because $N(s_i(x)) \le N(\Lambda)(x) < c'$, and on $O \cap \partial X$ every norm $N(s_i(y)) = N(\bar s_i(y))$ is below $c'$, so $N(\Lambda)(y) \le c' < c$. This is the only place where the tame strong bundle charts enter. Without them Definition 14.1.3 (1) constrains the $s_i$ only face by face, and nothing prevents $N(\Lambda)$ from jumping up along $\partial X$; the text uses the semicontinuity without comment.

*Construction of $g$ on $|X|$.* The orbit space $|X|$ is paracompact and Hausdorff. For an orbit $q$ let
$$t(q) = \{y \ge 0 : y < h(x) \text{ and, if } x \in C, \ y > N(\Lambda)(x), \text{ for every } x \text{ with } \pi(x) = q\},$$
an intersection of intervals, hence convex. Since $h$ and $N(\Lambda)|_{\partial X}$ are constant on orbits and $C$ is saturated, $t(q)$ is described by any one $x$ over $q$. Each $q = \pi(x)$ has a constant $c$ that lies in $t(q')$ for all $q'$ near $q$. If $x \in C$, take $c = (N(\Lambda)(x) + h(x))/2$: by the upper semicontinuity there is an open $O \ni x$ with $N(\Lambda) < c$ on $O \cap \partial X$, and $h > c$ on an open neighbourhood by continuity; if $x \notin C$, take $c = h(x)/2$ on $(X \setminus C) \cap \{h > h(x)/2\}$, open since $C$ is closed. In both cases the open set maps to an open neighbourhood of $q$, the orbit map being open (Proposition 7.1.8) ⟦Chapter7/Basic · EpGroupoid.proposition_7_1_8⟧, and the invariance of the data transfers the inequalities from the chosen representatives to all points of the orbits. Mathlib's selection lemma for convex-valued maps with local constant selections on a paracompact space ⟦Mathlib/Analysis/Convex/PartitionOfUnity · exists_continuous_forall_mem_convex_of_local_const⟧, a partition of unity argument, gives a continuous $g : |X| \to \mathbb R$ with $g(q) \in t(q)$ for all $q$: $0 \le g < h$, and $g > N(\Lambda)$ on $\pi(C)$.

*The Urysohn factor.* $\pi(C)$ is closed in $|X|$, $C$ being closed and saturated ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryCover · EpGroupoid.isClosed_π_image_of_isSaturated⟧, and $\pi(U)$ is open and contains it. By normality there is an open $V$ with $\pi(C) \subset V$ and $\operatorname{cl}V \subset \pi(U)$, and an Urysohn function $\psi : |X| \to [0, 1]$ with $\psi = 1$ on $\pi(C)$ and $\psi = 0$ off $V$.

*The functor.* $h_0 = (\psi g) \circ \pi$. It is continuous, non-negative, constant on orbits, hence a functor. Its topological support lies in $\pi^{-1}(\operatorname{cl}V) \subset \pi^{-1}(\pi(U)) = U$, $U$ being saturated. $h_0 \le g \circ \pi < h$, since $0 \le \psi \le 1$. On $C$, $\psi = 1$ and $h_0 = g \circ \pi > N(\Lambda)$. $\square$

*Tame strong bundle charts.* The proof uses a tame strong bundle chart at every object; it is proved from tameness of $X$ ⟦Chapter15/CutoffFunctor · SBLocalization.lemma_15_3_9_cutoff_hyp⟧ ⟦Chapter10/TameBundleCharts · SEP.exists_isTameChart⟧. Without tame bundle charts Definition 14.1.3 (1) would say nothing about the $s_i$, and $N(\Lambda)$ need not be upper semicontinuous along $\partial X$; the text uses the semicontinuity without comment. On $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0$ the cut-off statement holds with $h_0 = 0$, the boundary domain support being empty ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · SBLocalization.lemma_15_3_9_cutoff_statement_epSEP_zero⟧; with boundary and non-zero fibres it is the step (a) of the half-plane witnesses of §XV.27. Point G.177 is closed.

### XV.29 Proof of Theorem 15.3.9, steps (b)–(e): relative general position and the assembly

This subsection follows the proof of the theorem ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_9_of_rel⟧ after the cut-off of §XV.28, and then the proof of the relative statement that steps (c)–(d) supply.

**Step (b): the extension $\Lambda_0$.** Apply Theorem 14.1.9 (Part XIV) ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryExtensionTheorem · SBLocalization.theorem_14_1_9⟧ to $\Lambda$, with $\tilde U = U$ and the function $h_0$ of step (a). Its hypotheses are those of Theorem 15.3.9 together with the properties of $h_0$: $X$ tame, $|X|$ paracompact, partitions of unity, $\Lambda$ structurable over $\partial X$, $N$ an auxiliary norm, $U$ open, saturated and containing the boundary domain support, $h_0 \ge 0$ a continuous functor with topological support in $U$, and $N(\Lambda) < h_0$ on the boundary domain support ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryStructured · SBLocalization.theorem_14_1_9_statement⟧. It gives a structurable $\Lambda_0$ with $N(\Lambda_0) \le h_0$ everywhere, domain support in $U$, and $\Lambda_0 = \Lambda$ over $\partial X$. In particular $N(\Lambda_0) \le h_0 < h$ everywhere. *A reading*: after applying Theorem 14.1.9 the text writes "$N(\Lambda_0)(x) \le h(x)$"; presumably $\le h_0(x)$ is meant, since the margin $h_0 < h$ is what makes the final $N(\tilde\Lambda) < h$ strict.

**Step (c): general position of $\Lambda_0$ on the boundary.** For a level-$1$ point $x \in \partial X$ with $\Lambda_0(f(x)) > 0$, $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is reducedly onto at $x$. Indeed $f(x)$ lies over $x \in \partial X$, where $\Lambda_0 = \Lambda$, so $\Lambda(f(x)) > 0$; $x$ is a smooth point, every point of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$ being smooth under the tame germ data, since $f(x)$ is then the value of an sc⁺-section and $f - s$ is regularizing ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_smoothSet_of_pos⟧; and $\Lambda_0$ is an sc⁺-multisection on $W$ that agrees with $\Lambda$ over $\partial X$, so the face condition applies to it. The text says at this point that the boundary assumption gives, for every boundary point of the support, a neighbourhood $Q(x)$ on which the reduced linearization of $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is onto; the formal proof takes the condition at the boundary support points only and leaves the neighbourhood to the local families (below).

**Steps (c)–(d): the relative statement.** Its hypotheses hold for $\Lambda_0$: structurable, domain support in $U$, $N(\Lambda_0) < h$, reducedly onto at the boundary support points. It gives a structurable $\tilde\Lambda$ with $\tilde\Lambda = \Lambda_0$ over $\partial X$, domain support in $U$, $N(\tilde\Lambda) < h$, $(f, \tilde\Lambda)$ transversal and in general position. Its proof is the rest of this subsection.

**Step (e): the conclusions.** $\tilde\Lambda = \Lambda_0 = \Lambda$ over $\partial X$. Since $h \le 1$, $N(\tilde\Lambda) \le 1$, and with the domain support in $U \supseteq S_f$ the example after Definition 15.3.3 (§XV.18) makes $\tilde\Lambda$ admissible ⟦Chapter15/ControllingPairs · SBLocalization.isAdmissible_of_domSupp_subset⟧. The "moreover": general position, $N(\tilde\Lambda) \le 1$ and domain support in $U$ are the hypotheses of the "in particular" of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.21) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_in_particular_hyp⟧, which gives a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid with compact support. $\square$

The proof of the relative statement is "argue as in Theorem 15.3.7" (p. 698), made precise. The text takes parameter families $\Lambda_t$ with domain support away from $\partial X$ and asks of $\Lambda_t \oplus \Lambda_0$ near $(0, x)$, $x$ in the support, that the reduced linearization of the parametrized problem be onto. The formalization reads this as (15.1) relative to the branches of $\Lambda_0$, and then repeats the three parts of the proof of Theorem 15.3.7 (§§XV.22–XV.25): the assembly of local families, the parametric transversality, and the choice of $t$.

**Relative (15.1)** ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAtRel⟧. Let $t \mapsto \Lambda_t$, $t \in \mathbb R^\ell$, be a family of sc⁺-multisections and $x$ a level-$1$ point. The family satisfies (15.1) relative to $\Lambda_0$ at $x$ if it has, on an open $O \ni x$, a linear local representation by the sections $\sum_jt_js_{ij}$ with weights $\sigma_i$ independent of $t$ (as in §XV.22, ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.paramSec⟧), and for every operator $L$ in the support of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$, every chart presentation of $T^R_xX$ and every branch $i$, the map
$$T^R_xX \times \mathbb R^\ell \to W_x, \quad (v, \tau) \mapsto Lv + \sum_j\tau_js_{ij}(x)$$
is onto. The operators of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$ are the $(f - s^0_a)'(x)$ for the branches $s^0_a$ of $\Lambda_0$ through $f(x)$ (§XV.12); the text's (15.1) has the operators of $T(f, 0)(x)$, which is the case $\Lambda_0 = 0$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.isParamOntoAtRel_zero_iff⟧. Two properties. *Where $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is already reducedly onto*, relative (15.1) holds for every linear family, with $\tau = 0$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAtRel.of_reducedOnto⟧; this is how step (c) feeds steps (c)–(d) at the boundary support points. *Sums*: if one summand satisfies relative (15.1) and the other is linear, the sum family $\Lambda_t \oplus \Lambda'_{t'}$ on $\mathbb R^{\ell + \ell'}$ does ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAtRel.famAdd_left⟧ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAtRel.famAdd_right⟧. The sum family is linear with the branches $\sum_jt_js_{ij} + \sum_kt'_ks'_{i'k}$ on the intersection of the two domains ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.famAdd⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.isLocalRepOn_famAdd⟧; given $(v, \tau)$ for the branch $i$ of the first family, $(v, (\tau, 0))$ serves for every branch $(i, i')$ of the sum.

**Relative families** ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.RelGPFamily⟧. For a function $g$ and a set $Q$: a linear family ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.IsLinearFamily⟧ of structurable sc⁺-multisections with $N(\Lambda_t)(x) \le |t|_1\,g(x)$, domain support in a given $U'$, and relative (15.1) at every level-$1$ point of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f) \cap Q$. In the application $g = h - N(\Lambda_0)$ and $U' = U \setminus \partial X$. The family of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.22) is the case $\Lambda_0 = 0$, $g = h$. A family on $Q$ is one on every smaller set ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.RelGPFamily.mono⟧. Given a good system, the parameter-free family $\Lambda_t = 0$ ($\ell = 0$), which is linear ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.isLinearFamily_zero⟧ and structurable ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.isStructurable_zeroMultisection_of_goodSystem⟧, is a relative family on every set on which $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is reducedly onto at the support points ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.RelGPFamily.zeroOn⟧, in particular on $\emptyset$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.RelGPFamily.zero⟧. Two families on $Q$ and $Q'$ add to one on $Q \cup Q'$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.RelGPFamily.add⟧: the sum of structurable multisections is structurable (Proposition 13.3.12) ⟦Chapter13/StructurableMultisection · SBLocalization.proposition_13_3_12⟧; the norm of a sum is at most the sum of the norms ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.pointwiseNorm_multisectionAdd_le⟧, so $N \le (|t|_1 + |t'|_1)g = |(t, t')|_1\,g$; the domain support of a sum lies in the union ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.domSupp_multisectionAdd_subset⟧; and relative (15.1) passes to sums from either summand.

**Off the domain support.** An sc⁺-multisection is the zero multisection over every point outside its domain support ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.eq_zeroMultisection_of_notMem_domSupp⟧: in a local representation at that point every branch passes through the zero vector, for otherwise the point would be in the domain support, so $\Lambda(0_x) = \sum_i\sigma_i = 1$ and $\Lambda = 0$ elsewhere on the fibre. Hence $\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda = \Lambda_0$ over every point outside the domain support of $\Lambda$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeFamilies · SBLocalization.multisectionAdd_eq_left_of_notMem_domSupp⟧, the zero multisection being neutral for $\oplus$ (Part XIII) ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionNoInverse · SBLocalization.multisectionAdd_zeroMultisection⟧. This is what "domain support away from $\partial X$" achieves: $\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t = \Lambda_0$ over $\partial X$.

**The two analytic steps, relative to $\Lambda_0$**, as propositions. *The relative local families* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.HasLocalGPFamiliesRel⟧: every $x$ with $\Lambda_0(f(x)) > 0$ lies in an open saturated $Q$ carrying a relative family. *The relative parametric transversality* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.HasParametricGeneralPositionRel⟧: for every linear family of sc⁺-multisections with $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1(1 - N(\Lambda_0))$, domain support in $U$ and relative (15.1) at every point of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$, there is $\varepsilon > 0$ such that $(f, \Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t)$ is in general position for almost every $t$ with $\|t\| < \varepsilon$.

**The relative statement from the two steps** ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative_hyp⟧. Let $\Lambda_0$ be structurable with domain support in $U$ and $N(\Lambda_0) < h \le 1$, and assume the relative local families with domain support in $U \setminus \partial X$ and $g = h - N(\Lambda_0)$, and the relative parametric transversality. *Step 1, a good system.* A structurable $\Lambda_0$ carries a decoration, whose good system is all that the empty family needs. *Step 2, compactness of the support.* $|\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)|$ is compact ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.isCompact_image_supp_comp⟧: with $N(\Lambda_0) \le 1$ and domain support in $U$ this is the compactness of the "in particular" of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.21) ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.isCompact_orbitSupp_comp⟧, read as a statement about the image of $\{x : \Lambda_0(f(x)) > 0\}$ in $|X|$. *Step 3, from local to global* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.exists_relGPFamily_of_compact⟧. The images $\pi(Q_x)$ of the sets of the local families are open (Proposition 7.1.8) and cover the compact set of Step 2; finitely many suffice; the families on them add, by induction over the finite set starting from the empty family ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.nonempty_relGPFamily_biUnion⟧, to one relative family $F$ on a set $Q$ that contains $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$, the $Q_x$ being saturated. *Step 4, a regular parameter.* $F$ satisfies the hypotheses of the relative parametric transversality: its domain support lies in $U \setminus \partial X \subset U$, and $|t|_1(h - N(\Lambda_0)) \le |t|_1(1 - N(\Lambda_0))$ since $h \le 1$. So for some $\varepsilon > 0$, almost every $t$ with $\|t\| < \varepsilon$ gives general position. A ball has positive measure, so there is such a $t$ with $\|t\| < \min(\varepsilon, 1/(\ell + 1))$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.exists_norm_lt_of_ae⟧; in the sup norm of $\mathbb R^\ell$ this gives $|t|_1 < 1$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.sum_abs_lt_one⟧. *Step 5, the perturbation* $\tilde\Lambda = \Lambda_0 \oplus F_t$. It is structurable (Proposition 13.3.12). Over $\partial X$ it equals $\Lambda_0$: a point of $\partial X$ is not in the domain support of $F_t$, which lies in $U \setminus \partial X$. Its domain support lies in the union of the two, inside $U$. Its norm: with $m = h - N(\Lambda_0) > 0$ at $x$, $N(F_t)(x) \le |t|_1m < m$, so $N(\tilde\Lambda)(x) \le N(\Lambda_0)(x) + N(F_t)(x) < h(x)$, pointwise, without a uniform margin. It is in general position by the choice of $t$, hence transversal ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionAssembly · SBLocalization.isTransversal_of_isInGeneralPosition⟧. $\square$ Under the hypotheses of the relative statement the two steps give the relative statement ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative_of_hyp⟧; the relative parametric transversality is proved (below), so the relative local families alone suffice ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative_of_local⟧.

*Readings.* "Supported away from $\partial X$" is domain support in $U \setminus \partial X$ ($\partial X$ is closed and saturated). The text bounds the family by the norm of the perturbation without saying by what; the bound $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1(h - N(\Lambda_0))$ is the one that keeps $N(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t) < h$ for $|t|_1 < 1$ pointwise, and the parametric step needs only the weaker $|t|_1(1 - N(\Lambda_0))$. The text writes $\Lambda_t \oplus \Lambda_0$; the formalization adds in the other order, which changes nothing.

**The relative parametric transversality** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.hasParametricGeneralPositionRel⟧. For $X$ tame, $f$ an sc-Fredholm section functor with tame germ data, $(N, U)$ controlling compactness and $\Lambda_0$ an sc⁺-multisection with $N(\Lambda_0) \le 1$ and domain support in $U$, the relative parametric transversality holds. The compactness of $|S_f|$ is not used; that of $|\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)|$ comes from the control of compactness. The proof is that of the parametric transversality of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.25) with a branch $s^0_a$ of $\Lambda_0$ in place of the zero section, in four parts: one branch, the local step, the relative (15.2), and the assembly.

*One branch of $\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t$.* On a common open set $O$, local representations $(s^0_a, \sigma^0_a)$ of $\Lambda_0$ and $(s_i, \sigma_i)$ of $\Lambda$ give the representation of $\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda$ by the sections $s^0_a + s_i$ with the weights $\sigma^0_a\sigma_i$, reindexed by a finite type (Definition 13.2.9 (3)) ⟦Chapter15/RelativeBranch · SBLocalization.isLocalRepOn_multisectionAdd⟧: the sums are sc⁺ on $O$, the weights sum to $\sum_a\sigma^0_a\sum_i\sigma_i = 1$, and the count formula is that of the sum of Part XIII ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionAlgebra · SBLocalization.multisectionAdd_eq_sum_of_isLocalRepOn⟧. For a linear family the branch through $s^0_a$ and $\sum_jt_js_{ij}$ is the slice at $t$ of Part I's parametrized section on $\mathbb R^\ell \times O$,
$$F_{a,i}(t, y) = (f - s^0_a)|_O(y) + \sum_jt_j(-s_{ij})|_O(y),$$
that is, the slice at $t$ is $(f - (s^0_a + \sum_jt_js_{ij}))|_O$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeBranch · SBLocalization.paramSlice_branch_eq_rel⟧, a fibrewise identity in $W_y$ (the restricted sections being $f$ plus $(-1)$ times the section extended by zero off $O$).

*Relative (15.1) gives Part I's conditions (i)–(ii)* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeBranch · SBLocalization.isParamGP_restrictTo_of_onto_rel⟧. Let $c$ be a strong bundle chart of $W$ with domain in $O$, centred at $x$ ($c(x) = 0$), with $f(x) = s^0(x)$, $T$ a presentation of its model quadrant, and assume that $(v, \tau) \mapsto (f - s^0)'(x)v + \sum_j\tau_js_j(x)$ maps $T^R_xX \times \mathbb R^\ell$ onto $W_x$. Then in the transported chart of $W|_O$ the parametrized section $F$ has Part I's properties (i)–(ii) of the proof of Theorem 5.3.10 ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · StrongBundleChart.IsParamGP⟧. The proof reads everything in the chart. The linearization at $0$ of the principal part of $(f - s^0)|_O$ in the transported chart is the chart derivative $D((\hat f - \hat s^0) \circ r)(0)$ ⟦Chapter15/LocalIFT · SBLocalization.scDeriv_restrictTo_subRestrict⟧; the principal part of $-s_j$ at $0$ is minus the chart image of $s_j(x)$ ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.principal_eq_fiberLin⟧ ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.principal_restrictTo_negRestrict⟧; and the chart image of the intrinsic operator applied to $v$ is the chart derivative applied to the chart vector of $v$ ⟦Chapter15/BranchChart · SBLocalization.fiberLin_toIntr_subDeriv⟧. So the hypothesis says: for every $w$ there are $v \in T^R_xX$ and $\tau$ with $D(v) + \sum_j\tau_j\hat s_j(0) = \hat w$. (i), $F'(0, x)$ onto: given $k$ with $(0, k) \in K$, write $k = \hat w$ and take $u = v$ and $a = -\tau$, the sign coming from the $-s_j$. (ii), the kernel of $F'(0, x)$ is transversal to $\mathbb R^\ell \oplus T^R$: given a tangent vector $w$ at $0$, apply the hypothesis to the image $D(w)$; it gives $v \in T^R$ and $\tau$ with $D(v) + \sum_j\tau_j\hat s_j(0) = D(w)$; then $u = w - v$ has $D(u) - \sum_j\tau_j\hat s_j(0) = 0$, so $(\tau, u)$ is in the kernel, and $w - u = v \in T^R$. These are the two lines of linear algebra of the bridge of §XV.24, with $s^0$ for $0$.

*The parametrized branch is in general position* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeBranch · SBLocalization.isGeneralPositionGerm_branchParam_rel⟧. Let $(s^0_a, \sigma^0_a)$ represent $\Lambda_0$ on $O$, $s^0_a(x) = f(x)$, and assume relative (15.1) at $x$ for the sections $s_j$. Then $F_{a}(t, y) = (f - s^0_a)|_O + \sum_jt_j(-s_j)|_O$ is in general position at $(0, x)$ (Definition 5.3.9 on $\mathbb R^\ell \times O$). *Proof.* $x$ is smooth, being a zero of $f - s^0_a$ ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_smoothSet_of_eq⟧, so the tame germ data give a tame strong bundle chart centred at $x$; restrict it to $O$, and take a presentation $T$ of its quadrant. With this chart and the local structure $(s^0, \sigma^0)$ as local data, the intrinsic operator $(f - s^0_a)'(x)$ lies in the support of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$ ⟦Chapter15/LinearizationIntrinsic · SBLocalization.linearization_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter15/Transversality · SBLocalization.mem_support_linSumIntr_iff⟧, so relative (15.1) applies to it in this chart. The previous step gives (i)–(ii) in the transported chart; $(f - s^0_a)|_O$ is a tame sc-Fredholm section of $W|_O$ ⟦Chapter14/LocalScPlus · SBLocalization.isScFredholmSectionTame_subRestrict⟧ vanishing at $x$; and Part I's lemma that (i)–(ii) at a zero give general position of the parametrized section ⟦Chapter5/GeneralPosition · isGeneralPositionGerm_paramSection_of_isParamGP⟧ concludes.

*The local step* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.exists_local_paramGP_rel⟧. Let $X$ be tame, $\Lambda_0$ an sc⁺-multisection, $\Lambda_t$ sc⁺-multisections with relative (15.1) at $x_0$. Then there are an open $V \ni x_0$, $\delta > 0$ and a Lebesgue null set $C_V \subset \mathbb R^\ell$ such that $(f, \Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t)$ is in general position at every $y \in V$ with $(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t)(f(y)) > 0$, for $\|t\| < \delta$, $t \notin C_V$. *Proof.* Relative (15.1) provides a linear representation $\sum_jt_js_{ij}$ of the family on $O_1$; take a representation $(s^0_a, \sigma^0_a)$ of $\Lambda_0$ on $O_2$, its sections extended by zero, and $O = O_1 \cap O_2$; $O$ is a tame M-polyfold, an open subset of a tame one. For every pair $(a, i)$ there are an open $W_{a,i} \ni (0, x_0)$ in $\mathbb R^\ell \times O$ and a null set $C_{a,i}$ such that, for $(t, y) \in W_{a,i}$ with $f(y) = s^0_a(y) + \sum_jt_js_{ij}(y)$ and $t \notin C_{a,i}$, the branch $(f - (s^0_a + \sum_jt_js_{ij}))|_O$ is in Part I's general position at $y$. Two cases. If $f(x_0) = s^0_a(x_0)$, then $(0, x_0)$ is a zero of $F_{a,i}$, the slice at $t = 0$ being $f - s^0_a$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.sectionAdd_paramSec_zero⟧, $F_{a,i}$ is in general position there by the previous lemma, and Part I's local Sard step, the local form of Theorem 5.3.10 without compactness ⟦Chapter15/LocalSlice · StrongBundle.exists_null_isGeneralPositionGerm_paramSlice_near⟧, gives the neighbourhood and the null set; its slices are the branches. If $f(x_0) \ne s^0_a(x_0)$, the zero set of $F_{a,i}$, closed because $F_{a,i}$ is sc-smooth (Part I's Theorem 3.1.21) ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbationG · Polyfold.theorem_3_1_21_tame⟧ ⟦Chapter5/Transversality · isClosed_solutionSet⟧, misses $(0, x_0)$; its complement is $W_{a,i}$ and $C_{a,i} = \emptyset$. The finitely many $W_{a,i}$ contain a product $B_\delta(0) \times V'$, and $C_V = \bigcup_{a,i}C_{a,i}$ is null. For $y \in V'$, $\|t\| < \delta$, $t \notin C_V$ and $(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t)(f(y)) > 0$: $y$ is at level $1$; restrict a chart around $y$ to $O$; the sum representation has the branches $s^0_a + \sum_jt_js_{ij}$; each branch through $f(y)$ is in Part I's general position at $y$ by the choice of $W_{a,i}$; and Part I's general position of the branches through $f(y)$ is Definition 15.3.5 at $y$ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionLocal · SBLocalization.isReducedOntoAtLevel_of_isGeneralPositionGerm⟧ (§XV.16).

*Relative (15.2)* ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.exists_pos_supp_rel_subset⟧. If $(N, U)$ controls compactness, $N(\Lambda_0) \le 1$, the domain supports lie in $U$, $N(\Lambda_t) \le |t|_1(1 - N(\Lambda_0))$, and $V$ is a saturated open set containing $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$, then for some $\varepsilon > 0$ every $t$ with $|t|_1 < \varepsilon$ has $\operatorname{supp}((\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t) \circ f) \subset V$. The text's (15.2) has $S_f$; relative to $\Lambda_0$ the support of $\Lambda_0 \circ f$ takes its place. *Proof*, by contradiction. Suppose there are $t_n$ with $|t_n|_1 < 1/(n + 1)$ and $x_n \notin V$ with $(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_{t_n})(f(x_n)) > 0$. For $|t|_1 \le 1$, $N(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t) \le N(\Lambda_0) + |t|_1(1 - N(\Lambda_0)) \le 1$, so $x_n \in U$ and $N(f(x_n)) \le 1$ ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.auxLe_of_pos⟧, using the sum of multisections (Definition 13.2.9) ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionAlgebra · SBLocalization.definition_13_2_9⟧. The orbits $\pi(x_n)$ lie in the compact closure that the control of compactness provides; they have a cluster point $\pi(z)$. The object space is metrizable; along a decreasing basis $B_k$ of neighbourhoods of $z$ pick $n_k \ge k$ and $y_k \in B_k$ in the orbit of $x_{n_k}$ (the orbit map is open, so $\pi(B_k)$ is a neighbourhood of $\pi(z)$ met infinitely often). Then $y_k \to z$, $y_k \notin V$ ($V$ saturated), and $(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_{t_{n_k}})(f(y_k)) > 0$, since $f$ and the sum are functors. Decompose $f(y_k) = a_k + b_k$ with $\Lambda_0(a_k) > 0$ and $\Lambda_{t_{n_k}}(b_k) > 0$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.exists_of_multisectionAdd_pos_at⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ParamFamily · SBLocalization.exists_of_multisectionAdd_pos⟧; then $N(b_k) \le N(\Lambda_{t_{n_k}})(y_k) \le |t_{n_k}|_1 \le 1/(k + 1)$. Take a local representation $(s^0_i, \sigma^0_i)$ of $\Lambda_0$ on $O_2 \ni z$; for large $k$, $y_k \in O_2$ and $a_k = s^0_i(y_k)$ for some branch $i$; some $i$ occurs infinitely often, and along that subsequence $(f - s^0_i)(y_k) = b_k$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeParametric · SBLocalization.subSec_eq_of_add⟧. The section $f - s^0_i$ is continuous on $O_2$, being a tame sc-Fredholm section of $W|_{O_2}$, so $(f - s^0_i)(y_k) \to (f - s^0_i)(z)$; and $b_k \to 0_z$, because the $b_k$ lie in $W_{0,1}$, their base points converge to $z$ and their norms to $0$, which for an auxiliary norm forces convergence to the zero vector. Hence $f(z) = s^0_i(z)$, so $\Lambda_0(f(z)) > 0$ and $z \in V$; $V$ being open, $y_k \in V$ for large $k$, a contradiction. The text's analogue for $\Lambda_0 = 0$ is the statement (15.2) of §XV.25; the new point is the branch of $\Lambda_0$ met infinitely often.

*The assembly.* Let $S = \operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$. Every $z \in S$ is at level $1$ (§XV.20), so the local step applies at $z$ and gives $V_z$, $\delta_z$, $C_z$. $|S|$ is compact (Step 2 above); finitely many $\pi(V_z)$, $z \in T$, cover it. Let $V_s$ be the saturation of $\bigcup_{z \in T}V_z$, open and saturated, containing $S$. Relative (15.2) gives $\varepsilon_0$ with $\operatorname{supp}((\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t) \circ f) \subset V_s$ for $|t|_1 < \varepsilon_0$. Choose $\varepsilon > 0$ so that $\|t\| < \varepsilon$ implies $\|t\| < \delta_z$ for $z \in T$ and $|t|_1 < \varepsilon_0$. The union of the $C_z$, $z \in T$, is null. For $t$ off it with $\|t\| < \varepsilon$, and $x$ in the support of $(\Lambda_0 \oplus \Lambda_t) \circ f$: $x \in V_s$, so there are $y \in V_z$ for some $z \in T$ and a morphism $\varphi : y \to x$; the support is saturated, so $y$ is in it; general position holds at $y$ by the local step; and general position at a point is carried along morphisms ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPositionAt_of_mor⟧ (§XV.25). Pointwise general position everywhere on the support is general position ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionLocal · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPosition_iff_forall_at⟧. $\square$ The text discusses a countable cover and then remarks that a finite one suffices; the formal proof uses the finite cover directly, as in §XV.25.

**The relative local families** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · SBLocalization.relative_local_families_holds⟧ ⟦Chapter15/ChargedExtensionFibres · SBLocalization.relative_local_families_statement⟧. *Statement.* Under the hypotheses of the relative statement, every point of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$ lies in a saturated open set carrying a relative family with domain support in $U \setminus \partial X$ and bound $|t|_1(h - N(\Lambda_0))$. *Proof.* The statement splits in two ⟦Chapter15/RelativeReduction · SBLocalization.hasLocalGPFamiliesRel_of_boundary⟧: at the points of $\operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f) \cap \partial X$ the empty family suffices on a saturated open set where $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is reducedly onto at the support points; at the other points a relative family is built from seeds. *Boundary points* (step (c), the neighbourhoods $Q(x)$ of the text). General position of $(f, \Lambda_0)$ is open along the support ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · SBLocalization.exists_isOpen_isReducedOntoAtPt⟧: if every operator of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$ is onto on $T^R_xX$, the same holds at the support points near $x$, by the local step of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.25) with $\ell = 0$ parameters, where a Lebesgue null set of $\mathbb R^0$ is empty; the neighbourhood is made saturated by carrying general position along morphisms ⟦Chapter15/ParametricGeneralPosition · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPositionAt_of_mor⟧. *Interior points* (step (d), "argue as in Theorem 15.3.7"). The seeds relative to $\Lambda_0$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeedFamilies · SBLocalization.HasLocalParamSeedsRel⟧ ask, at $x \in \operatorname{supp}(\Lambda_0 \circ f)$, inside every open $O \ni x$ and below every $\varepsilon > 0$, finitely many sc⁺-sections $s_j$ supported in $O$ with $N(s_j) \le \varepsilon$ and an open $W \ni x$ on whose support points the relative (15.1) holds with the vectors $s_j(y)$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeedFamilies · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoVecRel⟧. They exist ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeeds · SBLocalization.hasLocalParamSeedsRel⟧: take a local representation $(s^0_a, \sigma^0_a)$ of $\Lambda_0$ at $x$ and global sc⁺-sections $\hat s_a$ equal to $s^0_a$ near $x$, $\hat s_a = \beta \cdot s^0_a$ for a bump function $\beta$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeeds · SBLocalization.exists_isScPlusSection_eqOn_nhds⟧; $f - \hat s_a$ is sc-Fredholm with tame germ data (Part I's Theorem 3.1.18) ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeeds · SBLocalization.isScFredholmSectionTame_subSec_univ⟧, and Part I's conditions (i)–(ii) for it are (15.1) for the operator $(f - s^0_a)'(y)$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeeds · SBLocalization.isParamGP_subSec_iff_onto⟧. For each branch through $f(x)$ Part I gives sections with (i)–(ii) at $x$ (§XV.23); the union of these finitely many families works for all the branches at once, and (15.1) is open along $\{f = s^0_a\}$ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeeds · SBLocalization.exists_open_onto_branch⟧; near $x$ every branch through $f(y)$ passes through $f(x)$. The atomic family of seeds, supported in a small neighbourhood avoiding $\partial X$ and scaled to the bound $|t|_1(h - N(\Lambda_0))$, is a relative family on a saturated open set ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeedFamilies · SBLocalization.exists_relGPFamily_of_seeds⟧, the relative (15.1) being carried along morphisms, as $T(f, \Lambda_0)(y)$ is the push-forward of $T(f, \Lambda_0)(x)$ (Theorem 15.1.1) ⟦Chapter15/RelativeSeedFamilies · SBLocalization.IsParamOntoAtRel.of_mor⟧; the good system that saturation needs is that of the decoration of the structurable $\Lambda_0$. $\square$ The proof uses partitions of unity only through their bump functions, and neither the paracompactness of $|X|$ nor the compactness of $|S_f|$ beyond the control by $(N, U)$: the relative statement from bump functions ⟦Chapter15/RelativeStepBump · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_relative_of_bump⟧ ⟦Chapter15/RelativeStepBump · SBLocalization.relative_local_families_of_bump⟧. *Witnesses* on the half-plane bundle of §XV.27, with the half-disc section and $\Lambda_0$ the multisection of the zero section: $\partial X \ne \emptyset$, non-zero fibres, every hypothesis of the relative statement, the boundary hypothesis at the support point $(0, 1)$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_7_relative_hypotheses_halfSEP⟧, the conclusion there ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_7_relative_halfSEP⟧, and the form from bump functions ⟦Chapter15/RelativeStepBump · HalfSpaceBundle.theorem_15_3_7_relative_of_bump_halfSEP⟧.

### XV.30 The cylinder $[0, 1]^n \times X$, and $d_X \equiv 0$ implies tame (prerequisites for Corollary 15.3.10)

Corollary 15.3.10 (p. 699) and its proof work on "the tame ep-groupoid $[0, 1] \times X$" with the pulled-back strong bundle $\widetilde W \to [0, 1] \times X$, whose fibre over $(t, x)$ is $W_x$, and the section $\tilde f(t, x) = (t, f(x))$. The text takes all of this for granted, and it assumes $d_X \equiv 0$ where Theorems 15.3.7 and 15.3.9, which it applies, ask for a tame $X$. This subsection builds the cylinder and proves the two facts the corollary uses silently: that the cylinder is a tame ep-groupoid with the expected boundary, orbit space and bundle, and that $d_X \equiv 0$ makes $X$ tame.

**The cube instead of the interval.** The formalization builds $[0, 1]^n \times X$ for every $n$, with Part I's cube $[0, 1]^n$ (the M-polyfold of maps $\{1, \dots, n\} \to [0, 1]$); the text's $[0, 1] \times X$ is $n = 1$. The reason is that Part I's parametrized Fredholm theory, which gives the sc-Fredholm property of $\tilde f$, is stated for the cube. Where the boundary or the time coordinate matters, $n = 1$ is fixed.

**The ep-groupoid** (extra) ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.cubeProd⟧. $[0, 1]^n \times X$ is the product (§XV.19) of the trivial ep-groupoid of the cube, whose only morphisms are identities, with $X$: objects $(q, x)$, morphisms $(q, \varphi)$, with source $(q, s(\varphi))$ and target $(q, t(\varphi))$. Two functors connect it with $X$. The projection $(q, x) \mapsto x$, $(q, \varphi) \mapsto \varphi$ is the second projection of the product ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.cubeProj⟧. The inclusion $i_q : x \mapsto (q, x)$, $\varphi \mapsto (q, \varphi)$ at a point $q$ of the cube ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.cubeIncl⟧ is sc-smooth because a constant map with a smooth value is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · MPolyfold.isScSmoothMap_const_of_mem_smoothSet_poly⟧ and every point of the finite-dimensional cube is smooth ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · cubePolyfold_mem_smoothSet⟧; and $\mathrm{pr} \circ i_q = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.cubeProj_comp_cubeIncl⟧.

*Tameness and the boundary.* If $X$ is tame, so is $[0, 1]^n \times X$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.isTame_cubeProd⟧, by Part I's tameness of products with the cube ⟦Chapter6/CubeSections · prod_cube_isTame⟧. The degeneracy index is additive, $d(q, x) = \#\{i : q_i \in \{0, 1\}\} + d_X(x)$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.degIndex_cubeProd⟧: the first term is the degeneracy index of the cube at $q$, and the sum is Part I's degeneracy index of a product of tame M-polyfolds. For $n = 1$ and $d_X \equiv 0$ the count runs over a one-element index set, so $d(t, x) = 1$ if $t \in \{0, 1\}$ and $d(t, x) = 0$ otherwise ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.degIndex_cubeProd_one_of_degIndex_eq_zero⟧. The boundary is the set of points of positive degeneracy index, so $\partial([0, 1] \times X) = \{0, 1\} \times X$ ⟦Chapter15/RegularPerturbations · SBLocalization.mem_boundary_cubeSEP_iff⟧. This is the fact behind "$\Lambda$ over $i = 0, 1$": the boundary data of Theorem 15.3.9 on the cylinder are data over the two ends.

*Isomorphism classes and the orbit space.* Since the cube contributes only identities, $(q, x)$ and $(q', x')$ are isomorphic iff $q = q'$ and $x$, $x'$ are isomorphic ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.cubeProd_π_eq_π_iff⟧; hence $S \times U$ is saturated whenever $U$ is ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.isSaturated_prod_cubeProd⟧. The map $(q, |x|) \mapsto |(q, x)|$ from $[0, 1]^n \times |X|$ to the orbit space of the cylinder is well defined by the same criterion ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.cubeOrbitMap⟧. It is continuous ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.continuous_cubeOrbitMap⟧: $\mathrm{id} \times \pi$ is an open quotient map (the orbit map of $X$ is open, Proposition 7.1.8), and composed with it the map is the orbit map of the cylinder, which is continuous. It carries $[0, 1]^n \times |A|$ onto $|[0, 1]^n \times A|$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.image_π_cubeProd⟧, so $|[0, 1]^n \times A|$ is compact when $|A|$ is ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.isCompact_image_π_cubeProd⟧. More is true. The map is injective ⟦Chapter15/Cylinder · EpGroupoid.injective_cubeOrbitMap⟧ (the isomorphism criterion again), surjective ⟦Chapter15/Cylinder · EpGroupoid.surjective_cubeOrbitMap⟧ (every orbit has a representative $(q, x)$), and open ⟦Chapter15/Cylinder · EpGroupoid.isOpenMap_cubeOrbitMap⟧: the image of an open $O \subset [0, 1]^n \times |X|$ is the image under the orbit map of the cylinder of the open set $(\mathrm{id} \times \pi)^{-1}(O)$, and the orbit map of the cylinder is open by Proposition 7.1.8. So it is a homeomorphism $[0, 1]^n \times |X| \cong |[0, 1]^n \times X|$ ⟦Chapter15/Cylinder · EpGroupoid.cubeOrbitHomeomorph⟧. A product of a compact space with a paracompact space is paracompact, and paracompactness is carried by homeomorphisms; so the orbit space of the cylinder is paracompact when $|X|$ is ⟦Chapter15/Cylinder · EpGroupoid.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_cubeProd⟧. This is the hypothesis "paracompact orbit space" of Theorem 15.3.9 on the cylinder, derived from the corollary's own hypothesis on $|X|$.

*Bump functions.* If $X$ admits sc-smooth bump functions (Definition 12.3.1, read pointwise), so does $[0, 1]^n \times X$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · EpGroupoid.admitsBumpFunctionsPointwise_cubeProd⟧: Theorem 12.3.2 (1) passes between the ep-groupoid and its object M-polyfold, and Part I proves bump functions on products with the cube ⟦Chapter6/CubeSections · admitsBumpFunctions_prod_cube⟧.

**The bundle and the section** (narrower). $\widetilde W$ is the pull-back of $W$ along the projection functor ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.cubeSEP⟧, Chapter 10's pull-back of a strong bundle over an ep-groupoid along a functor. A point of $\widetilde W$ is a pair $((q, x), w)$ with $w \in W_x$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.cubeVal⟧, and the morphism $(q, \varphi)$ acts by $\tilde\mu((q, \varphi), ((q, x), w)) = ((q, t(\varphi)), \mu(\varphi, w))$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeLift · SBLocalization.cubeVal_fibAct⟧. The section is $\tilde f(q, x) = ((q, x), f(x))$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.cubeLiftEp⟧, a section of $\widetilde W$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.isSection_cubeLiftEp⟧. The text writes $\tilde f(t, x) = (t, f(x))$ in the statement and $\tilde f(t, x) = f(x)$ in the proof; both mean this section.

*It is a functor* ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.isFunctorialSection_cubeLiftEp⟧: at a morphism $(q, \varphi)$ the identity $\tilde f(t(q, \varphi)) = \tilde\mu((q, \varphi), \tilde f(s(q, \varphi)))$ has first component $(q, t(\varphi))$ on both sides, and its second component is $f(t(\varphi)) = \mu(\varphi, f(s(\varphi)))$, the functor property of $f$. *It is sc-smooth* ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.isScSmoothSectionFunctor_cubeLiftEp⟧, by Part I's pull-back of sc-smooth sections along the sc-smooth projection. *It is sc-Fredholm when $f$ has Part I's tame germ data* ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_cubeLiftEp⟧: the germ of $\tilde f$ at $(q, x)$ is the germ of $f$ at $x$ with the cube coordinates as parameters, and Part I proves that this parametrized germ has tame germ data again ⟦Chapter6/CubeFredholm · StrongBundle.IsScFredholmSectionTame.paramCube⟧, which gives the sc-Fredholm property; the tame germ data of $\tilde f$ are recorded separately, for the theorems that need them on the cylinder ⟦Chapter15/RegularPerturbations · SBLocalization.isScFredholmSectionTame_cubeLiftEp⟧. *Its solution set* is $S_{\tilde f} = [0, 1]^n \times S_f$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.solutionSet_cubeLiftEp⟧, since the second component of $\tilde f(q, x)$ is $f(x)$ and the zero of $\widetilde W_{(q, x)}$ is $((q, x), 0_x)$; so $|S_{\tilde f}|$ is compact when $|S_f|$ is ⟦Chapter15/CubeEp · SEP.hasCompactSolutionSetEp_cubeLiftEp⟧, by the compactness of $|[0, 1]^n \times A|$ above.

*The added hypothesis.* The row is narrower only through the tame germ data of $f$, Part I's hypothesis for its parametrized Fredholm theory (Part I's untamed parametrized case is not formalized; with the 2021 untamed notion Part I's Theorem 3.6.8 does not hold as we read it, Part III). It is inherited, as in all of §15.2 (§XV.10), not new; its witness is the zero section of $\mathbb R^n \times \mathbb R^0 \to \mathbb R^n$ with its graph section's tame germ data ⟦Chapter15/InteriorPerturbationWitness · SBLocalization.theorem_15_2_2_hyp_witness⟧ ⟦Chapter4/NonVacuityG · isScFredholmSectionTame_graphSection⟧. The rest (tameness, boundary, orbit space, bump functions, compact solution set) holds as the text uses it.

**$d_X \equiv 0$ implies tame** (extra) ⟦Chapter7/DegeneracyZeroTame · EpGroupoid.isTame_of_degIndex_eq_zero⟧. *Statement.* An ep-groupoid whose degeneracy index vanishes at every object is tame; it is the statement for its object M-polyfold ⟦Chapter7/DegeneracyZeroTame · MPolyfold.isTame_of_degIndex_eq_zero⟧. Corollary 15.3.10 assumes $d_X \equiv 0$ and applies Theorems 15.3.7 and 15.3.9, which are stated for tame $X$; the text passes from one to the other without comment.

*Proof, the local step* ⟦Chapter7/DegeneracyZeroTame · isTameRetract_inter_of_degIndex_eq_zero⟧. Let $r : U \to U$ be an sc-smooth retraction of a partial quadrant $C \subset E$, presented by an sc-isomorphism $T : E \cong \mathbb R^k \times W'$ with $C = T^{-1}([0, \infty)^k \times W')$, and let $W$ be open with $d_C = 0$ on $r(U) \cap W$. The claim is that $r(U) \cap W$ is a tame retract (Definition 2.5.2). (i) For $z \in C$, $d_C(z) = 0$ iff all $k$ half-line coordinates of $T(z)$ are positive ⟦Chapter7/DegeneracyZeroTame · degIndexWith_eq_zero_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter7/DegeneracyZeroTame · degIndex_eq_zero_iff_pos⟧, and the set $D$ of such $z$ is open ⟦Chapter7/DegeneracyZeroTame · isOpen_setOf_pos_coord⟧, a finite intersection of open half-spaces. (ii) Restrict $r$ to $U' = U \cap r^{-1}(W) \cap D$. This is still an sc-smooth retraction: $U'$ is relatively open, $r$ maps $U'$ into $r(U) \cap W$, where $d_C = 0$, hence into $D$, and $r \circ r = r$. Its retract is $r(U) \cap W$: a point of $r(U) \cap W$ is fixed by $r$ and lies in $D$. (iii) Definition 2.5.2 (1), $d_C(r(x)) = d_C(x)$ on $U'$, holds because both sides are $0$ there. (iv) Definition 2.5.2 (2) asks, at a smooth point $x$ of the retract, for an sc-complement of $T_xO$ inside the minimal subspace $E_x$ of $E$ spanned by the directions in which no vanishing coordinate is tied down. Since no half-line coordinate of $x$ vanishes, $E_x = E$, and the range of $Dr(x)$ is sc-complemented in $E$ by $\ker Dr(x)$, Part I's sc-splitting of the range of the derivative of a retraction. Hence the restricted retract is tame.

*Proof, the M-polyfold.* At a point $x$ take a chart $\varphi$ whose degeneracy index at $x$ attains $d_X(x) = 0$ (Definition 2.4.1 takes a minimum over charts, and it is attained). The points of its domain with chart degeneracy index $0$ form an open set $s$, by the upper semicontinuity of the chart degeneracy index; the restriction of $\varphi$ to $s$ is again a chart of the structure, and its image is $r(U) \cap W$ for an open $W$. On it $d_C = 0$, so it is a tame retract by the local step, and the M-polyfold is tame by Part I's criterion that every point lie in a chart on a tame retract ⟦Chapter2/TameFaces · MPolyfold.isTame_of_forall_exists_chart⟧. The ep-groupoid is tame when its object M-polyfold is. Point G.183 is closed.

### XV.31 Corollary 15.3.10 (1) and (2) (2017: Corollary 15.1 (1), (2))

**The setting.** Corollary 15.3.10 (p. 699) assumes a strong bundle over an ep-groupoid with $d_X \equiv 0$ and paracompact orbit space $|X|$, and an sc-Fredholm section functor $f$ with $|S_f|$ compact. Part (1) produces a perturbation for one controlling pair, (2) an auxiliary norm on the cylinder that interpolates two controlling pairs, (3) a perturbation on the cylinder that interpolates two perturbations (§§XV.32–XV.33).

**Definition: regular perturbations** ⟦Chapter15/RegularPerturbations · SBLocalization.IsRegularPerturbation⟧. $(N, U, \Lambda)$ is a structurable regular perturbation of $f$ if $(N, U)$ controls the compactness of $f$ (Definition 15.3.1, §XV.17), $\Lambda$ is structurable (Definition 13.3.6, Part XIII), its domain support lies in $U$, $N(\Lambda)(x) < 1$ for all $x$, and $(f, \Lambda)$ is transversal (Definition 15.2.1, §XV.12). This is the text's definition in (1): "$T(f, \Lambda)(x)$ onto for all $x \in \operatorname{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)$" is transversality, and the clauses about $\Theta$ are a consequence, not part of the definition.

**Corollary 15.3.10 (1)** (explicit) ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · SBLocalization.corollary_15_3_10_1⟧. *Statement.* Let $d_X \equiv 0$, $|X|$ paracompact, $X$ admit sc-smooth bump functions, $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section functor with Part I's tame germ data and $|S_f|$ compact, and $(N, U)$ control the compactness of $f$. Then there is a $\Lambda$ such that $(N, U, \Lambda)$ is a structurable regular perturbation, $(f, \Lambda)$ is in general position (Definition 15.3.5), and $\Theta = \Lambda \circ f$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid with compact $|\operatorname{supp}\Theta|$. The proof composes the form with the statement of Theorem 15.3.7 as hypothesis ⟦Chapter15/Cylinder · SBLocalization.corollary_15_3_10_1_of_theorem_15_3_7⟧ with that statement for every bundle whose orbit space is paracompact ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_of_paracompact⟧ (§XV.26), which the corollary assumes.

*Proof.* (i) *Tameness.* $X$ is tame because $d_X \equiv 0$ (§XV.30). (ii) *Theorem 15.3.7 with $h \equiv 1$.* The constant function $1$ is continuous, constant along morphisms and satisfies $0 < h \le 1$; the statement of Theorem 15.3.7 (§XV.21) then gives a structurable $\Lambda$ with $N(\Lambda) < 1$, domain support in $U$, $(f, \Lambda)$ transversal and in general position. These are the clauses of a regular perturbation, with general position in addition. (iii) *The "in particular".* With $N(\Lambda) \le 1$, domain support in $U$ and general position, Theorem 15.3.7's "in particular" ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_in_particular_hyp⟧ (§XV.21) makes $\Theta$ a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid with compact support; its tame form ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_3_7_in_particular_tame_hyp⟧ gives the text's "tame branched". (iv) *Where the hypotheses enter.* $d_X \equiv 0$ is used only for (i), and paracompactness only through Theorem 15.3.7's structurable addendum, which needs a good system, supplied by Theorem 13.1.8 for paracompact $|X|$ (§XV.26). The text's proof says "a special case of Theorem 15.3.7"; the special case is $h \equiv 1$.

*Intermediate form.* The same conclusion with "$X$ tame" as an extra hypothesis in place of its derivation ⟦Chapter15/RegularPerturbations · SBLocalization.corollary_15_3_10_1_hyp⟧; the final form calls it with the tameness of §XV.30.

*Hypotheses not printed.* Sc-smooth bump functions, which Theorem 15.3.7 needs and the corollary does not state; Remark 15.3.2 says that the theory needs them. And the tame germ data of $f$, inherited from §15.2 (§XV.10). *Witness* ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · TrivialBundle.corollary_15_3_10_1_hypotheses_epSEP⟧ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · TrivialBundle.corollary_15_3_10_1_epSEP⟧: on $\mathbb R \times \mathbb R \to \mathbb R$ with $f(x) = (x, x)$ ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtensionWitness · TrivialBundle.diagSection⟧, the fibres are non-zero, $S_f = \{0\}$, every hypothesis holds with $(N, U)$ controlling compactness, and the corollary gives a regular perturbation. The corollary assumes $d_X \equiv 0$, so $\partial X = \emptyset$ by hypothesis.

**Corollary 15.3.10 (2)** (same) ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.corollary_15_3_10_2⟧. *Statement.* If $(N^0, U^0)$ and $(N^1, U^1)$ control the compactness of $f$, there are an auxiliary norm $\widetilde N$ of $\widetilde W \to [0, 1] \times X$ and a saturated open $\widetilde U \supset S_{\tilde f}$ such that $(\widetilde N, \widetilde U)$ controls the compactness of $\tilde f$ and restricts to $(N^i, U^i)$ over $t = i$. The text states (2) for regular perturbations $(N^i, U^i, \Lambda^i)$; the $\Lambda^i$ play no role in it, and only the control of compactness is assumed. The text does not give $\widetilde N$; its proof pulls back a single $N$, which is the first step below. The formalization proves both.

*The proof's step: one pair, pulled back.* $\widetilde N(q, x, h) = N(h)$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeAuxNorm⟧ is Part I's pull-back of an auxiliary norm along a map of the base ⟦Chapter5/CylinderPerturbation · StrongBundle.pullbackAuxNorm⟧; its value at a point of $\widetilde W_{0,1}$ is $N$ of the $W$-component ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeAuxNorm_eq⟧. It is an auxiliary norm of the strong bundle $\widetilde W$ (Definition 5.1.1) ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.isAuxNorm_cubeAuxNorm'⟧, by Part I's lemma that pull-backs of auxiliary norms are auxiliary norms ⟦Chapter5/CylinderPerturbation · StrongBundle.IsAuxNorm.pullback⟧. It is invariant under $\tilde\mu$ (Definition 8.3.5 (3)) ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.isAuxNorm_cubeAuxNorm⟧: if $h = \tilde\mu((q, \varphi), k)$, then the $W$-component of $h$ is $\mu(\varphi, k_2)$ and the cube coordinate is unchanged ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeVal_μ⟧, so $\widetilde N(h) = N(\mu(\varphi, k_2)) = N(k_2) = \widetilde N(k)$ by the invariance of $N$. At the values of $\tilde f$, "$\widetilde N(\tilde f(q, x)) \le r$" is "$N(f(x)) \le r$" ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.auxLe_cubeAuxNorm_iff⟧. Then $(\widetilde N, [0, 1]^n \times U)$ controls the compactness of $\tilde f$ whenever $(N, U)$ controls that of $f$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.controlsCompactness_cube⟧: $[0, 1]^n \times U$ is open, saturated (§XV.30) and contains $S_{\tilde f} = [0, 1]^n \times S_f$; and its sublevel set $\{z \in [0, 1]^n \times U : \widetilde N(\tilde f(z)) \le 1\}$ is $[0, 1]^n$ times the sublevel set $S$ of $(N, U)$. The orbit set of $[0, 1]^n \times S$ lies in the image of $[0, 1]^n \times \operatorname{cl}|S|$ under the continuous map $(q, |x|) \mapsto |(q, x)|$, a compact set, so its closure is compact ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.isCompact_closure_image_π_cube⟧.

*The weights.* $\alpha(s) = \min(1, 3(1 - s))$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeWeight₀⟧ and $\beta(s) = \min(1, 3s)$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeWeight₁⟧ are continuous and non-negative on $[0, 1]$, $\alpha = 1$ on $[0, \tfrac23]$, $\beta = 1$ on $[\tfrac13, 1]$, $\alpha(1) = 0$, $\beta(0) = 0$, and at every $s$ one of them is positive ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeWeight_pos⟧ (for $s \le \frac23$, $\alpha(s) = 1$; otherwise $\beta(s) = 1$). With the time coordinate $t(w)$ of a point of $\widetilde W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeTime⟧, a continuous function, the interpolated norm is
$$\widetilde N(w) = \max\big(\alpha(t(w))\,\widetilde N^0(w),\ \beta(t(w))\,\widetilde N^1(w)\big)$$
⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.interpAuxNorm⟧, with $\widetilde N^i$ the pull-back of $N^i$, and the interpolated set is $\widetilde U = ([0, \tfrac23) \times U^0) \cup ((\tfrac13, 1] \times U^1)$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.interpSet⟧.

*Step 1, a lemma on maxima.* For auxiliary norms $N_0$, $N_1$ of a strong bundle and constants $a, b \ge 0$, not both $0$, $\max(aN_0, bN_1)$ is an auxiliary norm ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · StrongBundle.isAuxNorm_max_mul⟧. If both are positive, Part I's lemmas that positive multiples and maxima of auxiliary norms are auxiliary norms apply; if $b = 0$, the maximum is $aN_0$, since $aN_0 \ge 0$, and symmetrically for $a = 0$.

*Step 2, $\widetilde N$ is an auxiliary norm of $(\widetilde W, \tilde\mu)$* ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.isAuxNorm_interpAuxNorm⟧. Definition 5.1.1 has three conditions and Definition 8.3.5 adds the invariance. (0) Continuity on $\widetilde W_{0,1}$: products and maxima of continuous functions. (1) On each fibre a complete norm: on the fibre over $z$ the time is the constant $t(z)$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.cubeTime_toOne⟧, so there $\widetilde N$ agrees with $\max(\alpha(t(z))\widetilde N^0, \beta(t(z))\widetilde N^1)$ for constants, which is a complete fibre norm by Step 1; completeness of a fibre norm depends only on the values on that fibre ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · StrongBundle.IsCompleteFiberNorm.congr⟧. (2) The convergence condition, that $w_k \in \widetilde W_{0,1}$ with $\widetilde P(w_k) \to z$ and $\widetilde N(w_k) \to 0$ converge to $0_z$. Let $t_0$ be the time of $z$; one of $\alpha(t_0)$, $\beta(t_0)$ is positive, say $\alpha(t_0)$. By continuity $\alpha(t(w_k)) > \alpha(t_0)/2$ for large $k$, so $\widetilde N^0(w_k) \le (2/\alpha(t_0))\,\widetilde N(w_k) \to 0$, and condition (2) for $\widetilde N^0$ gives $w_k \to 0_z$; symmetrically if $\beta(t_0) > 0$. (3) Invariance: both $\widetilde N^i$ are invariant, and $\tilde\mu$ does not change the time (the same lemma on the cube coordinate as above).

*Step 3, the restrictions.* Over $t = 0$, $\alpha = 1$ and $\beta = 0$, so $\widetilde N = \widetilde N^0$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.interpAuxNorm_of_cubeTime_eq_zero⟧, and $(0, x) \in \widetilde U$ iff $x \in U^0$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.mem_interpSet_zero⟧; over $t = 1$, $\widetilde N = \widetilde N^1$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.interpAuxNorm_of_cubeTime_eq_one⟧ and $(1, x) \in \widetilde U$ iff $x \in U^1$ ⟦Chapter15/CubeAuxNorm · SEP.mem_interpSet_one⟧. This is "restricts over $i = 0, 1$ to $N^i$, $U^i$".

*Step 4, $(\widetilde N, \widetilde U)$ controls the compactness of $\tilde f$.* $\widetilde U$ is open, a union of two products of open sets. It is saturated: each piece is a product of a set of times with a saturated set (§XV.30), and images and preimages under the orbit map commute with unions. It contains $S_{\tilde f} = [0, 1] \times S_f$: a point $(t, x)$ with $x \in S_f$ lies in the first piece if $t < \tfrac23$, since $S_f \subset U^0$, and in the second otherwise, since then $t > \tfrac13$ and $S_f \subset U^1$. For the compactness, let $(t, x) \in \widetilde U$ with $\widetilde N(\tilde f(t, x)) \le 1$; the value $f(x)$ then lies in $W_{0,1}$, the level of the pull-back being that of $W$. If $(t, x)$ is in the first piece, $\alpha(t) = 1$, so $N^0(f(x)) \le \widetilde N(\tilde f(t, x)) \le 1$ and $x$ lies in the sublevel set $S_0$ of $(N^0, U^0)$; in the second piece, $x$ lies in the sublevel set $S_1$ of $(N^1, U^1)$. So the sublevel set of $(\widetilde N, \widetilde U)$ lies in $([0, 1] \times S_0) \cup ([0, 1] \times S_1)$, whose orbit set has compact closure, by the pulled-back step applied to each piece. A closed subset of a compact set is compact.

*Reading.* The text's (2) asks for one pair restricting to the two given ones; it does not say how. The interpolation is ours; any partition of the time interval into two overlapping pieces works, and the margin between $\tfrac13$ and $\tfrac23$ makes $\widetilde U$ open while each end sees only one of the two pairs. With §XV.30 ($\tilde f$ sc-Fredholm with compact solution set, under the inherited tame germ data), all of (2) is covered.

### XV.32 Corollary 15.3.10 (3) (2017: Corollary 15.1 (3)): the glued boundary data and the partitions of unity of the cylinder

The proof of (3) (pp. 699–700) applies Theorem 15.3.9 on $[0, 1] \times X$ to the multisection over $\partial([0, 1] \times X) = \{0, 1\} \times X$ that is $\Lambda^i$ over $\{i\} \times X$, and concludes from its output. Theorem 15.3.9 (§XV.27) asks, of the space, that it be tame with paracompact orbit space and sc-smooth partitions of unity; and of the boundary data, that they be structurable over the boundary (Definition 14.1.7), have boundary domain support in $\widetilde U$, satisfy $\widetilde N(\Lambda) < h$ on the boundary, and satisfy the face condition. The text checks none of this explicitly. This subsection proves them for the glued data: tameness and paracompactness are in §XV.30; here are the glued data with their values, support, norm and charge, the partitions of unity of the cylinder, the structurability of the glued data and their face condition. Along this route a hypothesis on $X$ remains, sc-smooth partitions of unity, for Theorem 14.1.9 on the cylinder; the corollary itself is proved without it, by an explicit extension (§XV.33).

**The glued data** ⟦Chapter15/CylinderGlue · SBLocalization.cubeGlue⟧. $\Lambda_\partial(w) = \Lambda^1(w_2)$ if the time of $w$ is $1$, and $\Lambda^0(w_2)$ otherwise, $w_2$ being the $W$-component; so $\Lambda_\partial = \Lambda^0$ over $t = 0$ ⟦Chapter15/CylinderGlue · SBLocalization.cubeGlue_of_zero⟧ and $\Lambda^1$ over $t = 1$ ⟦Chapter15/CylinderGlue · SBLocalization.cubeGlue_of_one⟧. The values at times strictly between $0$ and $1$ are irrelevant: Theorem 15.3.9 looks at its boundary data only over $\partial([0, 1] \times X)$, and $\Lambda$ there is a function on all of $W$ of which only these values enter (§XV.27).

**Boundary domain support** ⟦Chapter15/CylinderGlue · SBLocalization.boundaryDomSupp_cubeGlue_subset⟧. If the domain support of $\Lambda^i$ lies in $U^i$, the boundary domain support of $\Lambda_\partial$, the closure of the set of boundary points $z$ over which some nonzero $w$ has positive weight, lies in $\widetilde U$. *Proof.* Let $z$ be such a point, $w \ne 0_z$ with $\Lambda_\partial(w) > 0$. By §XV.30, $z$ has time $0$ or $1$; say $0$. Then $\Lambda^0(w_2) > 0$ and $w_2 \ne 0$ in $W_{z_2}$, since $w$ and $((z, 0_{z_2}))$ differ only in the $W$-component ⟦Chapter15/CylinderGlue · SBLocalization.cubeVal_snd_ne_fiberZero⟧; so $z_2$ lies in the domain support of $\Lambda^0$. Hence the set lies in $(\{0\} \times \operatorname{dom-supp}\Lambda^0) \cup (\{1\} \times \operatorname{dom-supp}\Lambda^1)$, which is closed, so its closure does too; and a point of $\{i\} \times \operatorname{dom-supp}\Lambda^i$ lies in $\widetilde U$ by the restriction of $\widetilde U$ over $t = i$ (§XV.31).
